  Iktó thought that was fun, so he went to them, and he said to them, “little brothers, teach me that game.
  Míš-eyá waškáta wačhíŋ yeló!” eyé.
  I want to play it, too!” Čha uŋspékhiyapi yuŋkȟáŋ, “Hó, Iktó, níš heháŋtu weló,” eyápi.
  So they taught him and so they said, “well, Iktó, it’s your turn.” Čhaŋkhé ištá nuphíŋ glušlókiŋ na áta waŋkál yekíye.
  And so he took out both his eyes, and he sent them up.
  Hó éyaš ohíyiŋ kte ȟčiŋ čha líla eháš waŋkátuya yekíye.
  But he really wanted to win, so he threw them up extremely high.
  Khútakiya kú-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ čhaŋáletka waŋ ikȟóyakiŋ na hél otké!
  They were coming down, and so they got stuck in a tree, and they hung there.
  Héčheš Iktó waná ištá čhóla nážiŋ.
  It was like that so Iktó was standing without any eyes.
  Heháŋl itȟúŋkala kiŋ áta “Háŋta po!
  Then the mice said, “get away! Él uŋkáyapi kte ló!” eyápi na ábebeya napȟápi.
  He’s going to blame us!” and they scattered in all directions.
  Na waná Iktó ištáǧuŋǧa čha úŋšiya yutȟáŋtȟaŋ ománi.
  And now Iktó was blind, so he walked around feeling around pitifully.
  Wačhíŋyeye šni kiŋ uŋ khúl íyotakiŋ na čhéya-he.
  Since he was hopeless, he sat down and was crying. Éyaš ištá waníča čha ištámniyaŋpi čhóla, hó ečé uŋ čhéye.
  But he didn’t have any eyes, so without tears, he cried with just his voice.
  Iktó išnála yaŋká kéčhiŋ yuŋkȟáŋ uŋgná tuwá kȟaŋyéla heyé, “Tákuwe yačhéya-haŋ hwo?” Iktó thought he was sitting all alone, and so maybe someone nearby said, “why are you crying?” Čha “Huŋhuŋhí, misúŋ, othéȟike ló.
  So he said, “goodness, little brother, it’s really hard.
  Lé ištá mawániča čha wóyute kayéš iwágni owákihi šni.
  I don’t have any eyes, so I can’t even look for food. Čha akíȟ’aŋmat’iŋ kte ló!” eyé.
  So I’ll die of hunger!” Leyálaka hé Wanáǧi Itȟáŋčhaŋ kiŋ é čha kičhí wóglake.
  But actually, it was the Spirit Chief that he was talking with.
  Yuŋkȟáŋ heyé, “Hó čha čháŋ waŋ yuhá naŋké kiŋ hé ičú na makȟá kiŋ tȟáŋkaya yumímeya ičáǧo égle yo,” eyé.
  And so it said, “well take this stick that you’re sitting on and draw a big circle on the ground.” Ečhél ečhúŋ yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl Wanáǧi Itȟáŋčhaŋ kiŋ heyé, “Oháŋ čha čhaŋgléška waŋ yakáǧe kiŋ lé imáhel waúŋ kte.
  He did that, and so then the Spirit Chief said, “okay, so I will be in the circle that you drew.
  Yuŋkȟáŋ yutȟáŋtȟaŋ ománi na omáyuspe wačhíŋ yo.
  And so walk around touching things and try to catch me.
  Omáyaluspa oyákihi háŋtaŋš, ištá kiŋ iyékču kte ló,” eyé.
  If you can catch me, you will take back your own eyes.” Hó čha Iktó mahél iyáyiŋ na tȟokátakiya istó yuǧátiŋ na áta yutȟáŋtȟaŋ íŋyaŋke.
  So Iktó went inside, and he raised his arm forward, and he ran feeling around a lot.
  Na watúkȟa na themnít’a čhaŋna asníkiya íyotake.
  And he got worn out, and whenever he was sweating, he sat resting.
  Yámničhaŋ héčhuŋ na itópačhaŋ yuŋkȟáŋ nakéš Wanáǧi Itȟáŋčhaŋ kiŋ oyúspe!
  He did that for three days, and on the fourth day he finally caught the Spirit Chief!
  Wáŋčagna ištá kiŋ él kú na ečhél khíyotakiŋ na Iktó akhé taŋyáŋ wawáŋyaŋka škhé.
  All of a sudden, his eyes came back until they returned back, and Iktó saw well again.
  Ho hetáŋhaŋ tókhiya oyáte kiŋ wičhágnaye iyáye ka héči.
  And it’s a mystery as to where he went from there, tricking people.
  Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
  The end

7 – Iktómi na Wičhítegleǧa Há kiŋ Iktó wakpála čík’ala waŋ opȟáya ománi.
Iktó was walking along a small creek.
Oštéšteya hakítȟuŋ na wičhítegleǧa há waŋ siŋté kȟóya íŋ.
He was dressed weirdly, and he was wearing a raccoon skin tail over his shoulder, too.
Uŋgnáhela wakpá waŋ otȟáŋkaya na šmá čha ektá í, na óhuta él inážiŋ.
All of a sudden, he arrived at a wide and deep river, and he stopped at the edge. Óptakiya okíhi šni čha éna íyotakiŋ na čhéye.
He couldn’t go across it, so he sat back there, and cried.
Yuŋkȟáŋ čhetáŋ waŋ kiŋyáŋ hiyáya čha Iktó kipáŋ na “Hé, misúŋ, wáŋ, misúŋ!” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ, “Tókȟa hwo?” eyé.
And so a hawk went flying by, so Iktó called out to it, and he said, “hey, little brother, hey, little brother!” and so it said, “what’s wrong?” Čha heyé, “Misúŋ, amáyiŋ na uŋmá ečhíyataŋhaŋ éiȟpemaya yo!” eyé.
So he said, “little brother, take me and take me over to the other side!” Čha “Oháŋ” eyé.
So it said, “okay.” Yuŋkȟáŋ pahá tȟáŋka waŋ khiyéla háŋ čha čhetáŋ kiŋ ektá í na hekíye, “Hó, waná akáŋmayaŋka yo!
And so he was at a big hill, so the hawk got there to him, and it said, “well, now get on me!” Na sutáya mayúza yo – ničáitomni kte séče ’uŋ,” eyé.
And hold on tight – you might become dizzy.” Yuŋkȟáŋ Iktó, “Oháŋ, líla philámayaye ló, misúŋ,” eyá íyotake.
And so, sitting down, Iktó said, “okay, thank you, little brother.” Waná čhetáŋ kiŋ Iktó k’íŋ kiŋyáŋ yá yuŋkȟáŋ kȟoháŋ Iktó áta apȟóšiŋšiŋ na akát’apt’ápiŋ na ačhéžiyaŋpyaŋp yaŋké.
Then the hawk flew along with Iktó on his shoulders, and so in the meantime, Iktó was making faces, and sat snapping his fingers at him and sticking his tongue out at him.
Naȟmála ečhúŋ éyaš čhetáŋ kiŋ uŋgná naǧí kiŋ waŋkíglakiŋ na hečhíŋ, “Waȟtéšni kiŋ!
He did it in secret, but the hawk might have seen his shadow, and it thought, “piece of trash!
Akhé lé Iktó é yeló!
It’s Iktó again!
Tȟóš, čhaŋóȟloǧeča waŋží él oblúšna kte!” ečhíŋ.
First, I will drop him into a hollow tree.” Čhaŋóȟloka waŋ kál háŋ čha čhetáŋ kiŋ ékawiŋǧiŋ na áta iwáŋkab oȟ’áŋkȟoya iglúptaŋyaŋ čha Iktó mahél ohíŋȟpaye.
The hawk turned around at a log, and he flipped himself upwards really quickly, so Iktó fell into it.
Hél oyátȟakiŋ na čhéya-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ čhaŋkátotola waŋ wagnúka ewíčhakiyapi héčha čha líla pȟá lúta yuŋkȟáŋ čháŋ kiŋ él híyotake.
He was stuck there, so he was crying, and so there was a woodpecker, called a red-headed wookpecker with a red head that landed on the wood. Čha Iktó heyé, “Misúŋ, hél míčičaȟloka yo!” So Iktó said, “little brother, break a hole into this for me!” Čha katóto na kíčičaȟloka čha Iktó waná hetáŋhaŋ tȟaŋkátakiya étuŋwaŋ nážiŋ.
So it knocked on it, and made a hole in it for him, so then Iktó stood from there looking outside. Ȟčeháŋl wíŋyaŋ núŋm čhaŋk’íŋ hiyáyapi čha hečhíŋ, “Tókheškhe kaná wičháwagnayiŋ kte sél,” ečhíŋ.
Just then two women passed by gathering wood, so he thought, “maybe I should trick those ones somehow.” Yuŋkȟáŋ olówaŋ waŋ léčhel ahíyaye: And so he sings a song like this.
Hí - - hó!
Hí - - hó!
Hí - - hó!
Wičhítegleǧa čhépa lél maŋké ló!
A fat raccoon, here I sit.
Héčhel lowáŋ-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ wíŋyaŋ kiŋ uŋmá heyé, “Má, čépȟaŋši, wičhítegleǧa čhépa waŋ hél yaŋká kéye ’uŋ.” He was singing like that, and so one of the women said, “wow, cousin, it said that a fat raccoon is sitting here.” Heyá čha heháŋl Iktó iyótaŋ líla howáye!
She said that so then Iktó let out a loud cry!
Na sáŋm hótȟaŋkaya lowáŋ na leyé: And he sang even louder and he said, Wičhá čhépa lél maŋké ló!
A fat raccoon, I sit here!
Wičhá čhépa lél maŋké ló!
A fat raccoon, I sit here!
Yuŋkȟáŋ wíŋyaŋ uŋmá kiŋ, “Huŋhí, héčha líla oyúha waštépi ’uŋ, uŋkíču héči.” And so the other woman said, “goodness, this type of it must be easy to carry, if you and I take it.
Heyá yuŋkȟáŋ akhé Iktó, “Yutȟáŋkal imáču po, oyúha mawášte yeló!” eyé.
She said that and so Iktó said again, “if you take me out, I will be easy to carry!” Hó čha waná nazúŋspe ikíkčupi na čháŋ k’uŋ kaúŋkapi.
So then they took an ax and they cut down the tree.
Yuŋkȟáŋ wičhítegleǧa há šiná waŋ Iktó yuhá k’uŋ hé siŋté kiŋ oȟlóka ogná patȟáŋkal hiyúyiŋ na akhé šna yumáhel ičú-he.
And Iktó pushed out of the hole the tail of the raccoon skin blanket that he had, and again he was drawing back in often. Čha akhé wíŋyaŋ kiŋ waŋží heyé, “Čépȟaŋši, léna ȟčiŋ yaŋké kštó!
So one of the women said again, “cousin, it’s sitting right here!
Tóškhe ečhúŋk’uŋ kta he?” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ Iktó, “Pȟéta aúpi na ošóta-t’emáya po.
What are we going to do?” she said, and so Iktó said, “come here with fire and kill me with smoke.
Héčhel ečéla waglínaphiŋ kte ló,” eyé.
I will come out of here just in that way.” Čha akhíyapi ektá uŋmá gníŋ na pȟéta akú na uŋmá íŋš éna awáŋyaŋg nážiŋ kta kéyapi.
So one came back where they talk about it, and she was bringing fire, and the other one stood watching over it.
Hó éyaš Iktó heyé, “Hiyá – nuphíŋ yá po,” eyá čha héčhetulapi na nuphíŋ iyáyapi.
But Iktó said, “no – both of you go,” so they agreed and both left.
Kákhiya pahá aínab iyáyapi yuŋkȟáŋ Iktó tókȟašniyaŋ glinápȟe.
They went there to the other side of the hill, and so Iktó came out with no problem. Čháŋ waŋ ektá íŋyaŋkiŋ na héčhiyataŋhaŋ ahíyokas’iŋ na áta iȟát’e!
He ran to a tree, and he picked in from there, and he laughed hard.
Na heíč’iye, “Wíŋyaŋ henáuŋs nuphíŋ witkópi k’uŋ!
And he said to himself, “both of those women are fools!
Ognáye waštépi na míš miyé makhíli yeló!” eyé.
They were gullible and I’m really so good.
Waná wíŋyaŋ kiŋ nuphíŋ theȟíya pȟéta yuhá kúpi čha Iktó áta t’eblézešni iȟát’a áptaŋyiŋ na iglúš’iŋš’iŋ na iglúȟlaȟlate.
Now both the women were having trouble coming back with the fire, so Iktó rolled around laughing all crazy, and he tickled himself, and he scratched himself.
Yuŋkȟáŋ oígluspiŋ na čhaŋsákala eyá ičú.
And so he restrained himself and took some twigs.
Míla waŋ yuhá čha uŋ tokhéčhačha čhaŋsákala k’uŋ kažíbžipiŋ na étkiya wičháye.
He had a knife, so he was casually shaving the twigs, and he went towards them.
Iyékiyapi šni čha hewíčhakiye, “Tȟaŋkší, lé táku tókȟanuŋpi hwo?” They didn’t recognize him, so he said to them, “younger sisters, what are you two doing?” Heyá uŋkȟáŋ okíyakapi čha heyé, “Óčhičiyapi kte ló.
He said that, and so they said something, and he said, “I will help you two.
Wičhítegleǧa kiŋháŋ líla wígli óta yukȟáŋpi čha etáŋhaŋ wahíŋkpe isláye etáŋ omáyak’upi waštéke ’uŋ,” eyé.
Raccoons are really greasy so it would be good for you two to give me grease to oil my arrows with.” Waná čháŋ kiŋ áta oíleyapi na kabléblečapi éyaš tákuni ogná úŋ šni.
Then they set all the trees on fire, and they shattered them, but nothing was inside.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Iktó heyé: “Tȟaŋkší, wičhítegleǧa kiŋ líla wičhášapišni ’uŋ.
And so Iktó said, “younger sisters, raccoons are cunning.
Uŋgnáš pȟéta hiyóilalapi na kákhiya aínab ilálapi k’uŋ héhaŋ tokhíyotȟaŋ iyáyekiya načhéče ló,” eyé.
Maybe when you two left for fire and left to the other side over there, he ran away somewhere.” Heyíŋ na ȟeyáb inážiŋ na áta šiná óǧeya wičhákat’apt’ape.
He said that, and stood away from them, and he snapped at them through his blanket.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heyé, “Itȟó, eyá uŋgnáš čhaŋ-káksa omáwani kte,” eyíŋ na iyáye.
And so he said, “I guess, maybe I’ll walk chopping down trees,” and he left.
Na heíč’iye, “Wíŋyaŋ kiŋ henáuŋs áta nasúla waníčape ló!
And he said to himself, “those two women have no brains!
Wačhíŋ kiŋháŋ nuphíŋ tókȟašniyaŋ sáŋm wičháwagnayaŋ owákihi tkȟá.
When I want, I could trick both of them even more no problem.
Hó éyaš witkópi tkȟá nimákhiyapi čha eyá…héčhegla kte ló,” eyá škhé.
But they are fools, but they saved me so… that will be all.” Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

8 – Iktómi Natáhu Él Oyátȟag’ič’iye Iktómi tókhiya ománi yuŋkȟáŋ uŋgnáhela tuktél lowáŋpi na akíš’api čha naȟ’úŋ.
Iktómi was walking along somewhere, and so all of a sudden he heard that there was singing and jeering somewhere Čha anáǧoptaŋ nážiŋ yuŋkȟáŋ áta wačhí kiníča čha sičhúha kiŋ yašpúye.
So he stood listening to it, and so he felt like dancing, so his foot itched.
Tuktél oíyokiphiya wačhípi kiŋ olé.
He found where they were dancing happily.
Sáŋm hótȟaŋkaya lowáŋpi na akíš’api yuŋkȟáŋ khiyéla čhaŋkú aglágla tȟatȟáŋka pȟá šéča waŋ yaŋká yuŋkȟáŋ hetáŋhaŋ hiyú čha abléze.
The singing and was louder and so there was a dry buffalo skull close along the road, and so he realized it was coming from there.
Khúl iyúŋkiŋ na él éyokas’iŋ yukȟáŋ hená itȟúŋkalapi čha wačhípi na áta oíyokiphi tȟáŋka yuhápi!
He laid down, and he peered inside, and so he saw mice that were dancing, and they were having a lot of fun! Čha Iktó thiyópa katóto na heyé, “Misúŋ, úŋšimalapi na thimá ú iyówiŋmakhiya po!
So Iktó knocked on the door and said, “little brothers, be kind to me and let me come in!
Míš-eyá wawáčhi kte ȟčiŋ yeló,” eyé.
I really want to dance, too.
Hó čha “Wáŋ, thiyópa kiŋ čhiyé kiyúǧaŋ po!” eyápi.
So they say, “well, open up the door for our older brother!” Čhaŋkhé waná thiyópa lazátahaŋ kiŋ kiyúǧaŋpi.
And so they opened up the back door for him.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Iktó natá kiŋ tȟokéya mahél iyékiye éyaš heháŋyela okíhi šni.
And so Iktó stuck his hand in first, but he couldn’t go any further. Čha pašlóg-wačhíŋ éyaš átaya oyátȟake!
So he tried to take it off, but he was totally stuck!
Yuŋkȟáŋ itȟúŋkala kiŋ waŋží ité waŋyáŋkiŋ na, “Háŋta po!
Iktó é yeló!” eyá čha iyúha íŋyaŋg napȟápi.
And so one of the mice looked at his face, and it said, “look out!
It’s Iktó!” so they all left running away.
Yuŋkȟáŋ waná oíyokpaza čha Iktó áta ištáǧuŋǧe s’e wawáŋyaŋke šni.
And so then it was dark, so Iktó was looking around like he was totally blind.
Hó čha išnála čhaŋkú aglágla tȟatȟáŋka pȟá k’uŋ úŋ yaŋkíŋ na áta čhéye.
So he was sitting there alone along the path wearing the buffalo head, and he was really crying.
Tuwá khiyéla hiyáya naȟ’úŋ čháŋna líla hótȟaŋkaya čhéye.
Whenever he heard someone pass nearby, he cried very loudly.
Na iyópta iyáya čháŋšna akhé naȟmáȟma úŋšiya čhéye.
And whenever they went passing by, he sobbed softly and pitifully.
Hó yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl ták tókȟuŋ načhéče, éyaš táku čha ečhúŋ kiŋ ótakiya oyákapi.
And so then that may be what happened, but they tell what he did in many ways.
Uŋgnáš léčhuŋpi-s’e: Íŋyaŋ tȟáŋka waŋ khiyéla yaŋká yuŋkȟáŋ él í na “Tȟuŋkášila, ómakiya yo,” eyé.
Maybe it was just like this: a big rock sat nearby, and so he approached it, and he said, “grandfather, help me.” Čha íŋyaŋ kiŋ heyé, “Oháŋ, čha létkiya íŋyaŋkiŋ na natá kiŋ kóskos hiyúya yo,” eyá ayúpte.
So the rock replied, “okay, so run over here, and swing your head.” Čha Iktó héčhuŋ naháŋ íŋyaŋ kiŋ íčat’a kahíŋȟpeyiŋ na tȟatȟáŋka pȟá kiŋ áta kabléče.
So Iktó did that, and he ran into the rock really hard, and the buffalo skull completely shattered. Éyaš iyé natá kiŋ kȟó iwóič’ito čhaŋkhé natá ksúyeič’iye.
But he bumped himself against his head too, and so he hurt his head. Čha áta kaítomni na tȟéhaŋ owáŋžila yaŋkíŋ na gléglepa-he.
So he got really dizzy, and he sat still for a long time, and he flew up over and over again.
Naíŋš húŋȟ léčhel oyákapi: Tȟatȟáŋka pȟá uŋ hé wakpá él oíȟpeye-šípi čha ečhúŋ.
Or some tell it like this: he was told to throw the buffalo skull into the river so he did it. Éyaš iyé kȟó oíȟpeič’iyiŋ na ečhél mnít’e séče.
But he also threw all of himself into it until he might have drowned.
Naíŋš uŋgnáš léčhetu: Iktó čhethí na owátohaŋyaŋ tȟatȟáŋka pȟá kiŋ pȟéta kiŋ iwáŋkab yúziŋ na ečhél líla ǧuíč’iya škhé.
Or maybe it’s like this: Iktó built a fire, and he held it over the fire until he burned himself.
Héčhekčhe oštéšteya wičhóoyake kiŋ lé oyákapi.
They tell the story in many different and odd ways.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

9 – Anúŋg Ité Waŋ Wikȟóškalaka Waŋ Gnáye Eháŋni oyáte waŋ wičhóthi yuŋkȟáŋ él wikȟóškalaka waŋ wíŋyaŋ wašté čha thí na líla waštélakapi.
A people from long ago lived in a village, and so a pretty young woman lived there, and they really liked her.
Waúŋšila na nakúŋ ksápa čha wičháša kiŋ iyúha asčúpi na kičhí úŋpi čhíŋpi éyaš iyé wičháčhiŋ šni.
She was compassionate, and also smart, so all of the men were attracted to her, and they wanted to live with her, but she didn’t want them.
Yuŋkȟáŋ aŋpétu waŋ él kȟoškálaka waŋ líla ówaŋyaŋg wašté čha tókhiyataŋhaŋ hí.
And so one day, a really good-looking young man from somewhere else arrived there.
Wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ líla waštélakiŋ na hiŋgnáyaŋ čhíŋ kéye.
The young woman really liked him, and she said that she wanted to marry him.
Hó éyaš, húŋku na atkúku kiŋ owíčhakiyake šni.
But she didn’t tell her mother and her father. Ée kȟoškálaka kiŋ kičhí naȟmála iyáyiŋ kta iglúštaŋ na héčhetkiya iglúwiŋyeya.
Instead she decided to leave in secret with the young man, and she got ready over there.
Waná haŋčhókaŋ háŋl hiyóhi.
Then, when it was the middle of the night, he came for her.
Wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ čhápala waŋ waníyaŋpi yuhá čha ikíkču na hinápȟe.
The young woman took a beaver she had as a pet, and she left.
Kȟoškálaka kiŋ thilázata apȟé nážiŋ yuŋkȟáŋ kičhí iyáye.
The young man was waiting behind the tipi, and so she went with him.
Líla yápi na blé waŋ šmá čha él ihúŋnipi.
They went a ways, and they reached a deep lake.
Yuŋkȟáŋ kȟoškálaka kiŋ, “Hó, nuŋwáŋ iblúweǧiŋ kta čha čhuwí akáŋl ímayotaka yo,” eyé.
And so the young man said, “well, I’ll swim across it, so sit on my back.” Éyaš wikȟóškalaka kiŋ ečhúŋ čhíŋ šni kéye.
But the young woman said that she didn’t want to do it.
Yuŋkȟáŋ wáŋčag kȟoškálaka kiŋ áta čhaŋzékiŋ na íšiŋkčhiŋ heyé, “‘Él íyotaka yo,’ epȟé!
And so all of a sudden the young man got really angry, and he angrily said, “I said, ‘sit on it!’ Ečhánuŋ šni kiŋháŋ mni-máhel iȟpéčhiyiŋ kte ló!” eyé.
If you don’t do that, I’ll throw you into the water!” Waná wikȟóškalaka kiŋ líla kȟokípȟa čha ečhél ečhúŋ, agná nuŋwáŋpi uŋspé šni kiŋ hé uŋ.
Then the young woman was really scared, so she did that, since moreover she hadn’t learned how to swim.
Waná nuŋwáŋ yápi na wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ čhuwí akáŋl yaŋkíŋ na nažúte kiŋ él étuŋwaŋ yaŋké.
Then they went swimming, and the young woman was sitting on his back, and she sat looking at the nape of his neck.
Yuŋkȟáŋ abléziŋ na héčhiyataŋhaŋ nakúŋ ité yukȟé.
And so she noticed something, and there was a face, too.
Lé “anúŋg ité” héčha itéke!
He must have been “double face!” Eháŋuŋ, kȟoškálaka waŋ waštélake k’uŋ hé lé é šni kiŋ nakéš abléziŋ na líla waȟtélašni na nakúŋ líla glá.
Indeed, she realized that this wasn’t the young man that she liked, and she really hated him, and also absolutely detested him.
Iyúweǧapi na khiyéla čhaŋmáhel wakhéya waŋ líla tȟáŋka čha etáŋhaŋ šóta izíta-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ él ípi.
They crossed, and they arrived at a big tent near the forest that had smoke coming out of it.
Heháŋl wičháša kiŋ, “Maȟwá yeló.
Then the man said, “I’m tired.
Heyómičile yo, kȟoháŋ mištíŋmiŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyé.
Look to see if I have lice, I’ll be sleeping in the meanwhile.” Čhaŋkhé waȟtélašni uŋ áta alóslos hiŋglé éyaš kȟaŋyéla hiyúŋka čha heyókičile na ečhél yuíštiŋme.
And so she felt immense disgust towards him, but she laid down near him, and she searched for lice until she made him fall asleep.
Yuŋkȟáŋ matȟápiȟ’a eyá líla čikčík’alapi čha opúza čhaŋkhé pȟehíŋ kiŋ égna psípsičapi!
And so he was infested with what were small little toads, and so they jumped throughout his hair. Čha íŋyaŋ blaská núm ičú na matȟápiȟ’a waŋží oyúspa čháŋšna íŋyaŋ kiŋ okógna égnakiŋ na šna íŋyaŋ kiŋ íčhiyapȟa na ečhél kat’é.
So she took two flat rocks, and whenever she caught a toad, she laid it between the rocks, and she often bumped the rocks together until it died.
Anúŋg ité kiŋ waná mahétuya ištíŋme.
Double Face was in a deep sleep.
Kiksúyešniyaŋ ȟpáye kiŋ heháŋl, pȟehíŋ líla háŋskaska yukȟé kiŋ húŋȟ ičú na léčhel yuhá škáŋ: Then he was laying uncouscious, she took some of his really long hairs, and she used it like this: Tȟošú waŋ él iyákaškiŋ na akhé húŋȟ tȟošú tȟókeča waŋ ektá éiyakaškiŋ na héčhel iyáza áye.
She tied it to a tipi pole, and she tied it to some other tipi poles, and she went form one to another.
Héčhuŋp-s’e thiyókawiŋȟ glihúŋni čha iktómi tȟawókaške s’e híŋ na čhokáŋyaŋ anúŋg ité k’uŋ ȟpáye.
She went around the tipi just like that, so it sat like a spider web, and Double Face sat in the middle.
Wáŋčagna čhápala k’uŋ ikíkču na thiyátakiya íŋyaŋg gličú.
Immediatley she took her little beaver and she started to run back home.
Waná thíkhiyela kú yuŋkȟáŋ mní šmé k’uŋ glakíŋyaŋ ȟpáya čha ektá khihúŋni na tókȟa iyúweǧe šni.
Then she was coming close back to her home, and so she arrived back at the deep water lying across there, and she couldn’t cross it. Čha éna íyotakiŋ na líla čhéye.
So she sat back there, and she really cried.
Héčhena čhápa kiŋ čháŋ óta yaksáksa škáŋ naháŋ íyečhana čheyáktȟuŋpi waŋ kaȟ-yúštaŋ.
The beaver kept working biting down a lot of trees, and soon afterwards he finished making a dam. Čha él alí gliyúweǧapi.
So they crossed by walking on it.
Uŋmá ečhíyatahaŋ waná makȟá alípi yuŋkȟáŋ tuwá wičhílazata páŋpaŋ ú.
They walked on the earth to the other side, and so someone was coming yelling behind them.
Wikȟóškalaka kiŋ hakíkta uŋkȟáŋ anúŋg ité k’uŋ hé é čha áta napé apáhaha étkiya íŋyaŋke!
The young woman turned around, and so it was Double Face that was running towards her with his hand out. Íŋš-eyá čheyáktȟuŋpi k’uŋ hé alí-wačhíŋ éyaš líla očík’ala kiŋ uŋ iwáŋyaŋg ȟ’aŋhíya ú.
He was trying to walk on the dam too, but since it was narrow, he was coming carefully and slowly.
Héčheča čha kȟoháŋ čhápa kiŋ étkiya yužúžu áye.
It was like that so in the meanwhile the beaver was taking it apart.
Anúŋg ité kiŋ čhokáta í ȟčeháŋl čheyáktȟuŋpi kiŋ okápsagyiŋ na ohíŋȟpayiŋ na mnít’e.
Right when Double Face got to the middle, it struck off the dam, and he fell in, and he drowned.
Héčhena wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ čhápa oglúspiŋ na líla íŋyaŋg gníŋ na thiyáta khí.
The young woman kept holding the beaver, and she went back really running, and she got back home.
Hél waŋglákapi eháŋl áta ok’óke.
When they saw her there, there was a lot of commotion.
Huŋkáke kiŋ líla wíyuškiŋpi na nakéš kȟoškálaka waŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ é kúŋze k’uŋ hé hiŋgnáye.
Her parents were really happy, and finally she married the young man that Double Face pretended to be. Čhápala uŋ hé theȟílapi na taŋyáŋ yuhápila čha hetáŋhaŋ áta waštéič’ila škhé.
They loved that little beaver, and they kept him, and from that it thought very highly of itself.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

10 – Anúŋg Ité Wikȟóškalaka waŋ Manúŋ Eháŋni, oyáte tȟáŋka waŋ wičhóthi yuŋkȟáŋ égna wikȟóškalaka waŋ witȟáŋšna úŋ čha él thí.
A long time ago, a large group of people were camped, and so she lived as a single woman.
Líla wíŋyaŋ wašté na wakáȟ wóphika čha wičháša kiŋ iyúha yúzapi čhíŋpi.
She was a very pretty woman, and she was skilled at making things, so all the men wanted to take her.
Hó éyaš waŋžíni wičháčhiŋ šni.
But she didn’t want anybody.
Wičháša óta ókšaŋtaŋhaŋ oyúspe hípi khéš átaš él éwičhatuŋwe šni.
Whenever many men came around to court her, she didn’t look at them at all.
Yuŋkȟáŋ haŋhépi waŋ él, akhé kȟoškálaka kiŋ ektá ípi na okhíyapi.
And so one evening, the men came by again, and they courted her.
Waŋží wókiyag nážiŋ na uŋmá kiŋ hená enána apȟé yaŋkápi.
One stood talking, and others sat back there waiting.
Yuŋkȟáŋ ȟčeháŋl itókaǧataŋhaŋ kȟoškálaka tȟókča waŋ ú.
And so just then one other young man was coming from the south.
Kákhiya tȟéhaŋl ú kiŋ héhaŋni líla waštémna hiyú čha wíŋyaŋ k’uŋ áta t’íŋzani nážiŋ okíhi šni s’eléčheča.
When he was coming from very far away, good smelling odors came from him, so the woman felt like she couldn’t stand still.
Na agná kȟoškálaka waŋ kičhí wóglaka-he uŋ hé átaš éwačhiŋ šni.
And moreover she didn’t pay any attention to the young man that was talking with her.
Ečháŋleš waŋží ú k’uŋ hé ečéla ayúta-he.
Instead she was only looking at the one that was coming.
Waná khiyéla ú yuŋkȟáŋ áta wakšúpi eyála s’e iglúziŋ na pȟehíŋ háŋskaska na tókhel okíhika ówaŋyaŋg wašté.
Then he was getting close, and so he was wearing nothing but beadwork, and his hair was really long, and he was as good-looking as could be.
Hihúŋni kta háŋl sémni waŋ wíŋyaŋ kiŋ wókiyaka-he uŋ hé iyénayiŋ na ȟeyáb iyáye.
When he was arriving, the young man that had been talking to the woman gave up, and he went away.
Kȟoškálaka waŋ ú k’uŋ wikȟóškalaka kiŋ aóhomni iyáyiŋ na itȟókab hinážiŋ.
The young man who was coming went around the young woman, and he arrived in front of her.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heyé, “Hó, wíŋyaŋ, nišnála waštéčhilake uŋ, tȟéhaŋtaŋhaŋ wahí yeló.
And so he said, “hey, woman, since I like you alone, I came from very far away.
Uŋgníŋ kta čha čhihíyowahi yeló.
You and I will go back, so I came here for you. Čha iyówiŋyaye háŋtaŋš, mihákab ú wo,” ekíye.
So if you agree, come back with me.” Héčhegla waéyiŋ na khiglá uŋkȟáŋ wáŋčagna ihákab iyáye.
That is all he said, and he left and so immediately she followed after him.
Héčhuŋ čha kȟoškálaka uŋmá k’uŋ hená iyúha inážiŋpi na owátohaŋyaŋ étkiya étuŋwaŋ nážiŋpi.
She did that, so all the other young men stopped, and they stood watching her for a short time.
Na haŋkéya kibléčahaŋpi na thípi ektákta glápi.
And finally they scattered, and they went back to their tipis.
Yuŋkȟáŋ, haŋhépi átaya wikȟóškalaka kiŋ kȟoškálaka kiŋ lé kičhí ománi.
And so all night the young woman was walking along with the young man.
Waná líla tȟéhaŋl ihúŋnipi uŋkȟáŋ heyé: Then they arrived a long way off, and so she said, “Yú!
Ečéš, ȟ’aŋhíya máni ye!
Líla nilúzahaŋ čha watúkȟamayaye kštó,” eyé. “Gosh!
Gosh, don’t walk fast!
You walk really fast, so you are tiring me out.” Yuŋkȟáŋ wičháša kiŋ íšikčhiŋ, “Hóȟ, iníla khéš ináȟni yo!
And so the man said angrily, “goodness no, hurry up quietly instead! Áŋpawi kiŋ hináphiŋ kta čha wakhíhuŋni kte héčha yeló,” eyé.
The sun will come up, so I must get back.” Čha wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ blihéič’iyiŋ na ihákab máni.
So the young woman gathered her courage, and walked following him.
Yuŋkȟáŋ waná wí hináphiŋ kta wahéhaŋl kȟoškálaka uŋ hé ayúta uŋkȟáŋ haŋhépi hiyói uŋ hé é šni.
And so then around the time when the sun was rising, she looked at that young man, and so in the evening he was not the one that had taken her.
Wakšúpi na pȟehíŋ na wókȟoyake waštéšte uŋ hená iyúha tókȟaȟ’aŋ.
All the nice beeded things, and hair, and costumes were gone. Ée wičháša kiŋ akȟóketkiya máni éyaš nakúŋ ahítuŋwaŋ ú s’eléčheča.
Instead, the man walked with is back to her, but it was like he was coming looking at her.
Anúŋg ité eyápi héčha!
He was the one called Double Face! “Yá!” wikȟóškalaka kiŋ eyíŋ na éna patȟág inážiŋ. “Ah!” the young woman said, and she stopped back there.
Wáŋčagna anúŋg ité k’uŋ kawíŋȟ hiyú na uŋžíŋžiŋtka hú waŋ yužúŋ.
Immediately Double Face turned around, and he pulled out wild roses.
Yuhá áta kasáksakiŋ na pasí áye.
He whipped her with it, and he marched ahead of her.
Na wakpála čhúŋšoke waŋ opȟáya yápi uŋkȟáŋ čhaŋmáhel šóta izíta čha étkiya pačékček áye.
And they were going along the forest, and so he pushed her towards something smoking in the woods.
Yuŋkȟáŋ, hél wizíla – thiíkčeka tȟaŋníla – waŋ háŋ čha thiyópa kiŋ él inážiŋpi.
And so, they arrived at the door of an old smokey tipi.
Waná wičháša kiŋ leyé, “Hó, thimá iyáya yo!” eyá čha thimíyaye.
Then the man said, “hey, go inside!” so she went inside.
Heháŋl čhatkúta wikȟóškalala núm yaŋkápi čha waŋwíčhayaŋke.
Then she saw that two young women were sitting at the back of the tipi.
Ité na napé kiŋ áta oíyokšilyela ȟaŋȟáŋpi.
Their sad faces and hands were full of scabs.
Yuŋkȟáŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ; “Hó, iyáyiŋ na hená ób íyotaka yo.
And so Double Face said, “okay, go and sit with them.
Hená íŋš-eyá waštéič’ilapi čha awíčhawagli yeló!” eyá čha ób íyotake.
They thought highly of themselves too, so I brought them here!” he said, so she sat with them.
Thičhókata očhéthi waŋ yaŋkíŋ na él čhéǧa ogná táku píǧiŋ na áta anágloglo-he.
She sat in the middle around the fire, and there was a kettle boiling something, and it was bubbling.
Yuŋkȟáŋ etáŋhaŋ wičhánape waŋ nawáŋkal hiyú!
And so a human hand rose from in it.
Na thiyópa ikȟáŋyela winúȟčala waŋ yaŋká yuŋkȟáŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ hekíye: And an old woman sat near the door, and so Double Face said to her, “Uŋčí, lená waná eyáš špáŋ načhéče ’uŋ. “Grandma, maybe these are cooked too much. Čha imíčiču wo.
So get it for me.
Wikȟóškalaka kiŋ lená ób wátiŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyá čha kazé na wakšíča tȟáŋka waŋ ožúye, waháŋpi kȟóya.
I will eat these young women,” so she served a dish, and she filled a big bowl, including soup.
Na anúŋg ité kiŋ wók’u čha waháŋpi iyáyustag yúte.
And she fed Double Face, so he ate it together with the soup.
Iglúštaŋ na heháŋl wikȟóškalaka kiŋ wóyapte kiŋ wičhák’u.
He finished it, and then he gave the women the leftovers.
Wičálapi šni éyaš uŋžíŋžiŋtka hú k’uŋ hé uŋ áta wičhákasaka čha waháŋpi na tȟaló kiŋ hená yútapi.
They refused it, but he whipped them with the rose stalks, so they ate the soup and the meat.
Hó, wól iglúštaŋpi uŋkȟáŋ heháŋl anúŋg ité kiŋ iglútȟaŋ.
So, they finished eating, and so then Double Face got all dressed up.
Akhé hayápi waštéšte eyá uŋ šna wíŋyaŋ wičhágnaye uŋ hená kič’úŋ na phiyá wičháša wašté ič’íčhaǧe.
He put on the nice things that he had often tricked the girls with, and he turned into a good-looking man again.
Heháŋl winúȟčala uŋ hé khíŋ na, “Hó, Uŋčí.
Then he addressed the old woman and said, “well, grandma.
Akhé ománi mníŋ kte ló.
I will leave walking around again.
Kȟoháŋ lé wikȟóškalaka waŋ eháke awágli kiŋ lé kté na pȟá kiŋ kaksá yo!
In the meantime, kill the last woman that I brought back here, and cut off her head!
Tókša wátiŋ kte ló,” eyíŋ na tokhíyotȟaŋ iyáye.
I’ll surely eat it,” he said, and he left to somewhere.
Yuŋkȟáŋ winúȟčala uŋ hé hewíčhakiye, “Tȟakóža, wikȟóškalaka waštéšte léčhel ečé awíčhagli na šna wičhákte na wičháyute.
And so the old woman said to them, “grandchildren, he only brings pretty women like this back, and usually he kills them, and he eats them. Čha takómni niyé kȟó niyútapi čhíŋ!
So he certainly wants to eat you all too!
Hó éyaš, tȟakóža, nitȟéčapi kštó.
But you, granddaughters, you all are young.
Na miyéš makáŋ ye.
And I’m old myself.
Hó čha maktépi na líla napȟá khiglá pe,” eyé.
So kill me, and run back to where you came from,” she said.
Heháŋl leyé; “Kákhiya čhaŋmáhel čhaŋwákakse s’a kiŋ héčhiya nazúŋspe čík’ala mitȟáwa kiŋ hé ičú pe.
Then she said, “take that small ax of mine there where I often cut down trees.
Yuŋkȟáŋ mitȟáŋčhaŋ kiŋ iyáyustag iȟpéyapi na ináȟni napȟá pe!” eyé.
And so throw it away with my body and hurry away!” Čha waná witȟáŋšna úŋ waŋ eháke aglípi k’uŋ hé inážiŋ na winúȟčala kiŋ kat’é.
So the last woman to have arrived stood up and she killed the old woman.
Héčhuŋ na waéčhuŋ-ší uŋ hená ečhékčhe ečhúŋ.
She did that, and since she told her to do things, she did them like that.
Yuŋkȟáŋ iyúha líla khiglápi.
So all of them really went back to where they had come from.
Tónačhaŋ heháŋyaŋ líla thiyátakiya íŋyaŋkapi.
They sprinted towards home for a couple days.
Hetáŋhaŋ yámni-čhaŋ yuŋkȟáŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ khihúŋni na tȟaló tȟéča kiŋ iyútȟa čhíŋ.
Three days later, Double Face arrived back, and he wanted to taste the fresh meat. Ȟčeháŋl čhaŋmáhel tuwá čhaŋ-kátoto-haŋ čha ektá hóyeye; “Uŋčí, waná kú na lená imíčiȟ’aŋ yo!
Just then somebody was knocking on wood, so he yelled out, “grandma, come now and cook me something!
Wátiŋ kte ló,” eyé.
I’m going to eat.” Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Tȟakožá, híŋyaŋka.
And so someone responded, “grandson, wait a minute.
Waná čónala ihéwakiya čha waglúštaŋ na heháŋl wakú kte ’uŋ,” eyá ayúpte.
I will finish the little that I have left to do, and then I’ll be coming back.” Héčhena čhaŋzékiŋ na, “Wáŋ, Uŋčí, ináȟni-čhiší yeló!
He kept being angry and he said, “hey, grandma, I told you to hurry up!
Na ináyaȟni šni háŋtanaš tȟebčhíyiŋ kte ló!” eyé.
And if you don’t hurry up, I will eat you up!” Heyá uŋkȟáŋ winúȟčala k’uŋ hé akhé hóuye; “Híŋyaŋka, Tȟakóža.
He said that, and so the old woman said again, “wait a minute, grandson.
Waná léčheglala čha waglúštaŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyé.
Now this is all so I’ll be finished.” Anúŋg ité uŋ waná áta čhaŋzégzeg hiŋgníŋ na čhaŋyátakiya yé éyaš kȟúŋšitku tȟaŋčháŋ kiŋ ečéla t’á ȟpáya čha iyéye.
Double Face then got really angry, and he went towards trees, but he found just his grandmother’s dead body.
Wáŋčagna, “Waȟtéšni šíčapi kiŋ!
Immediately he said, “pieces of trash!
Uŋčí míktepi na tókhel iyáyapi ’uŋ!” eyíŋ na thi-ánataŋ na míla tȟáŋka okíle.
They killed my grandmother and left somehow!” and he charged towards the house and looked for his knife.
Iyékiyiŋ na héčhena wikȟóškalaka kiŋ oyé otȟáb wičhákhuwa.
He found it and he kept chasing after the young women’s tracks. Áta líla lúzahaŋ čha wáŋčag tókȟašniyaŋ wičhákigleǧe!
He ran super fast so he had no problem catching up with them.
Wikȟóškalaka eháke k’uŋ hakíkta uŋkȟáŋ waná anúŋg ité kiŋ toháŋyela ú na leyáya íŋyaŋke: The last young woman turned around, and so Double Face was coming from there, and he ran saying, “Waȟtéšni kiŋ! “Piece of trash!
Ináȟmemayákhiyapi okíhipi šni!
They can’t hide you from me!
Makȟá kiŋ níškola yeló!” eyíŋ na áta míla kpazózo ú čha wikȟóškalaka eháke k’uŋ áta čhéya íŋyaŋke.
The world is only so big!” and he came showing off his knife, so the last young woman ran along sobbing. Ȟčeháŋl itȟókab blé waŋ líla tȟáŋka čha yaŋká čha óhuta kiŋ él inážiŋ.
Just then, she came up to the shore of a really big lake that was there.
Yuŋkȟáŋ čhokáta thípi máza waŋ háŋ čha waŋyáŋke.
And so she saw that in the middle there was an iron house.
Yuŋkȟáŋ etáŋhaŋ wičháša waŋ líla háŋska čha hinápȟe.
And so a really tall man came out of it.
Wíŋyaŋ uŋ nihíŋčiya kipáŋ; “Wičháša, ómakiya ye!
The woman yelled out in fear, “sir, help me!
Táku waŋ makté kte ’uŋ!” eyé.
Something is going to kill me!” Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Oháŋ, tȟoéyaš takúmayayiŋ kta hwo?” eyá ayúpte.
And so he responded, “okay, but first, what will your relationship to me be? Čhaŋkhé, “Íŋ, hiŋgnáčhiyiŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ wičála šni.
And so she said, “uh, I’ll marry you,” and so he refused. “Hiyá, tóškhe takúmayayiŋ kta hwo?” eyá čha wótakuye eyá yukȟé kiŋ iyúha čhažéyate. “No, how is it that you will be related to me?” he said, so she named all the kinship relations that people can have. Óhaŋketa, “Atéčhiyiŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ “Háu, Čhúŋkš, héčhetu weló.
Finally she said, “you will be my father,” and so he said, “yes, daughter, that’s how it is. Čha mní kiŋ alí hiyú wo,” eyé.
So come walking across the water.” Yuŋkȟáŋ mní kiŋ alíli íŋyaŋkiŋ na thípi kiŋ él ihúŋni.
And so she ran on the water, and she arrived at the house.
Heháŋl nakéš waná anúŋg ité k’uŋ mnióhuta él hinážiŋ.
Then finally at that moment Double Face got to the edge of the water.
Sáŋm hiyú okíhi šni čha aglágla glakíŋkiŋyaŋ íšiŋkčhiŋ máni-he.
He couldn’t come any further, so he angrily walked along the edge of the water from side to side.
Wičháša kiŋ heháŋl leyé, “Hó, Čhúŋkš, thimá níŋ kte éyaš waktá yo.
Then the man said, “Well, daughter, you were going inside, but wait.
Wamákȟaškaŋ tób wičhábluha yeló.
I have four animals.
Thiyópa kiŋ itȟáŋnuŋg igmú tȟáŋka waŋ é na matȟó waŋ kičhí ȟpáye ló.
On the doors on both sides lay a cougar and a bear.
Na thičhókab zuzéča tȟáŋka waŋ yuŋké.
And in the middle of the house there’s a big snake.
Na thičhátku kiŋ hél íŋš tȟatȟáŋka waŋ ȟpáye ló,” eyé.
And there’s a buffalo at the honor place.” “Čha thimá ilániŋ kte kiŋháŋ, ‘Até thimá hiyú-maší ye,’ eyá yo. “So when you go inside, say, ‘my father told me to come in.’ Na zuzéča waŋ ȟpáye kiŋ akȟótaŋhaŋ, tȟatȟáŋka waŋ ȟpáye kiŋ itȟáhena, hél iyókogna íyotaka yo,” eyá čha ečhékčhe ečhúŋ.
And the snake that’s laying on the other side, and the buffalo that’s laying on this side, sit down between them,” he said, so she did it like that.
Wičháša kiŋ íŋš-eyá thimá gličú, na thiyópa máza kiŋ ečhél iyéye.
The man was going back inside, and he closed the iron door. Ȟčeháŋl anúŋg ité kiŋ tókheškhe hiyú wélakȟa hihúŋni na thiyópa kiŋ katóto.
Just then Double Face, evidently having come somehow, arrived there, and he knocked on the door. “Wičháša šíča, wíŋyaŋ kiŋ hé hiyúmičičhiya yo!” eyá čha, “Iyáya yo! “Evil man, hand me that woman!” he said, so he said, “leave!
Mičhúŋkši kiŋ ayúštaŋ yo!” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ héčhena áta čhaŋzéke.
Leave my daughter alone!” and so he kept being really angry.
Míla tȟáŋka waŋ yuhá k’uŋ hé uŋ thiyópa kiŋ tókhel okíhika aphíŋ na kašpú.
He banged his knife that he had on the door as much as he could, and he knocked it loose.
Hó čha wičháša waŋ máza thípi othí uŋ hé é čha heyé, “Igmú, matȟó, wíŋyeya ȟpáya po!” eyé.
So the man who lived in the iron house said, “cougar, bear, lie ready!” Na waná wahúkheza waŋ uŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ khiŋíŋ.
And then he threw a lance at Double Face.
Yuŋkȟáŋ makhú ot’íŋs aphíŋ na ȟčeháŋl igmú na matȟó k’uŋ áta iyáȟpayapi na ktépi.
And so it hit him right in the chest, and just then the cougar and bear attacked him, and killed him. Čha wičháša uŋ hé anúŋg ité k’uŋ yuslóhaŋ áyiŋ na makȟóblaye waŋ él éuŋpe.
So that man dragged Double Face away, and he laid him in a flat meadow.
Na heyé, “Hó, Čhuŋkší, čhaŋšéča óta tȟokšú aglí na akšú na ačhéthi yo.
And he said, “well, daughter, come back carrying dried wood, and pile it on him, and light a fire on it.
Na él waŋyáŋg nážiŋ yo.
And stand looking at him.
Naháŋ táku wóyuha waštéšte hetáŋhaŋ napsípsil hiyú kte éya waŋžíni ičú šni yo,” eyé.
And don’t take any one of the nice items that will spring out of there. “Waŋží uŋgná iyáču kiŋháŋ, šičáya kte ló!” eyé.
If you take one, it will be bad!” he said. Čha wikȟóškalaka k’uŋ waná ečhél ečhúŋ.
So then the young woman did that.
Yuŋkȟáŋ anúŋg ité kiŋ ȟugnáȟ áye kiŋ ečhél, táku waštéšte pȟéta kiŋ etáŋhaŋ napsípsil hiyú – wizípȟaŋ naíŋš takúku wíŋyaŋ tȟa-wóyuha héčha.
And so while Double Face was burning up, many nice items sprung out from the fire – satchels or items that are women’s things.
Yuŋkȟáŋ tȟahíŋšpa waŋ uŋ háŋpa kaǧéǧepi s’a kȟá čha líla wašté yuŋkȟáŋ napsíl hiyú.
And so she remarked a very nice awl to make moccasins out of that sprung out from it.
Héčhuŋ na wikȟóškalaka kiŋ itȟókab kawóslal glihé.
It did that and it stood in front of the young woman.
Yuŋkȟáŋ atkúku kiŋ héčhuŋ šni ší éyaš núŋǧe wanílya ičú.
And so her father told her not to do that, but she took it without listening. Á oȟláthe yumáhel ičú yuŋkȟáŋ héčhena tȟahíŋšpa k’uŋ áta čhuté él ičhápȟe!
She took it under her armpits, and so the awl accidently stabbed her under the arm.
Anákiȟma uŋ-wáčhiŋ éyaš waná líla yazáŋ na išíl áya čha áta t’íŋ kte s’eléčheča.
She tried to hide it, but then it hurt a lot, and she was becoming sick because of it, so she felt like she was going to die.
Hó čha héčhetu khéš ogláke: “Até, tákuni ičú šni-mayáši k’uŋ, tȟahíŋšpa waŋ iwáču.
So this being the case she said, “father, you telling me not to take anything, I took an awl. Čha waná uŋ mat’íŋ kte s’eléčheča ’uŋ,” eyé.
So now I feel like I’m going to die.” Čha heyá ayúpte, “Háu, hé wičháša kiŋ nikté čhíŋ yuŋkȟáŋ waná witkóya oyáȟ’aŋ čha héčhel óyakiye ló!” ekíye.
So he responded, “yes, that man wants to kill you, and so you took that action so you helped him like that.” Yuŋkȟáŋ matȟó kiŋ aphíye-ší.
And so he told the bear to cure her. Ȟnáȟna ohítiya škáŋ éyaš áta otúyačhiŋ čha haŋkéyaš iyénaye.
He worked ferociously while grunting, but it was absolutely in vain, so he gave up. Čha heháŋl igmú tȟáŋka uŋ hé iyútȟe eyáš íŋš-eyáš okíhi šni.
So then the cougar tried, but he couldn’t either.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl tȟatȟáŋka kiŋ aphíye-wačhíŋ na akhé okíhi šni.
And so then the buffalo tried to heal her, and he couldn’t again.
Hó heháŋl zuzéča k’uŋ hečéla okápte.
And then the snake was the last one left.
Waná owótȟaŋla ȟčiŋ iyúŋkiŋ na á kiŋ yapȟé.
Then he laid down straight up, and he held his mouth to her armpit. Ȟčeháŋl tȟatȟáŋka kiŋ ópta iyáyiŋ na zuzéča uŋ siŋté kiŋ alí.
Just then the buffalo went by, and he stepped on the snake’s tail. Čha áta čhaŋzé hiŋgníŋ na wikȟóškalaka kiŋ wanúŋ yaȟtáke!
So it got really angry, and he bit the young woman on accident!
Yuŋkȟáŋ á kiŋ líla pó hiyúyiŋ na etáŋhaŋ tȟahíŋšpa uŋ hé naslúta čha akísni.
And so a lot of puss came from her armpit, and the awl came out, so she was cured.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl wičháša kiŋ heyé, “Hó, Čhúŋkš, toháŋyaŋ yačhíŋka lél úŋ wo.
And so then the man said, “well, daughter, stay here as long as you want.
Na toháŋl iyónikiphi na yaglá yačhíŋ kiŋháŋ ogláka yo,” eyé.
And if you are happy and you want to go back home, tell me.” Tȟéhaŋke s’e yuŋkȟáŋ watóhaŋl líla thiyáta glá čhíŋ čha atkúku kiŋ okíyake.
It seemed like some time and then at one point she wanted to go back home, so she told her father that.
Yuŋkȟáŋ matȟó kiŋ é na igmú tȟáŋka k’uŋ kičhí akhíȟpeye-wičháši.
And so he told the bear and the cougar to go with her. “Thi-ótȟaŋiŋyaŋ éiȟpeyapi na gličú po,” eyé. “Drop her off in sight of her tipi and come back,” he said.
Hó čha matȟó igmú kičhí wikȟóškalaka kiŋ waná aglápi na thi-ótȟaŋiŋyaŋ inážiŋpi.
So the young woman went was going back with the bear and the cougar, and they stopped in view of her tipi.
Hetáŋhaŋ išnála gníŋ na thiwégna khí čha áta oíyokiphi tȟáŋka.
From there she was going back alone, and she arrived on the outskirts of the tipis, so she was really happy.
Heháŋl wamákȟaškaŋ kiŋ kawíŋǧapi na íŋš-eyá thiyátakiya khiglápi škhé.
Then the animals turned around, and they started back on their way home, too.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

11 – Anúŋg Ité waŋ é na Čhiyékičhiyapi Yámni kiŋ Kȟoškálaka yámni čhiyékičhiyapi čha kál thípi.
Three young men were brothers to each other so they lived over there.
Wakhúl wóphikapi na nakúŋ waókapi čha yuphíya tákuni ičákižešniyaŋ úŋpi.
They were skilled in hunting, and they were good marksmen, so they lived not suffering from lack of anything.
Hó éyaš waŋžíni tȟawíčutȟuŋ šni.
But not one of them were married.
Yuŋkȟáŋ aŋpétu waŋ él, wíŋyaŋ waŋ ȟtayétu eháŋl tȟaŋkál hinážiŋ na pȟámahel ičhóma nážiŋ.
And so one day, a woman was standing outside when it was evening, and she sat with a blanket wrapped on her head. Ȟčeháŋl wóta-haŋpi čha tȟokápȟa kiŋ heyé, “Wáŋ, thimá kičhó po!
Just then they were eating, so the eldest one said, “hey, invite her in! Íŋš-eyá wótiŋ kte ló,” eyé.
She’ll eat, too. Čhaŋkhé Hakéla ináphiŋ na thimáhel ú-ší.
And so the youngest one went out and asked her to come inside.
Yuŋkȟáŋ iníla ȟčiŋ híyotake.
And so she sat down all quietly.
Wók’upi éyaš iníla ȟčiŋ ičú na tákeye šni wóte.
They gave her things to eat, but she took them quietly, and she ate not saying anything.
Tokȟáš waŋží hiŋgnáyiŋ kta hí séča uŋkȟáŋ tȟéhaŋ él úŋ éyaš héčhuŋ kta awáčhiŋ šni čha ablézapi.
Maybe she had come to marry one of them, and so she was there for a long time, but they realized that she wasn’t thinking of doing that.
Heháŋl kȟokípȟa áyapi na, “Uŋgnáš lé uŋktépi čhíŋ na uŋ hí séče ló!” ekíčhiyapi.
Then they were getting scared, and they said to each other, “maybe she wants to kill us, and that might be the reason she came!” Yuŋkȟáŋ híŋhaŋni waŋ él, ománi yápi kta uŋkȟáŋ Hakéla léčhel ekíyapi: “Hó, Hakéla, aŋpétu átaya thiákaŋl yaŋkíŋ na awáŋyaŋka yo.
And so one morning, they were going to walk around, and so they told Hakéla, “hey, Hakéla, sit on the tipi all day, and stand watch.
Na uŋglípi kiŋ táku tókȟa háŋtaŋš oyáka yo,” eyápi.
And when we come back, if something’s the matter, say so.” Čha waná čhiyéku kiŋ iyáyapi čha ób iyáye kúŋziŋ na ečháŋleš héktakiya glí.
So then the younger brothers left, so he pretended to leave with them, but instead he came back.
Thiíkčeya tȟošú kiŋ waŋží alí na íŋkpata yaŋké.
He climbed up one of the tipi poles, and he sat at the top.
Wičhéškoȟloka kiŋ ogná thimá éyokas’iŋ na wákhil yaŋké.
He peered inside through the smoke hole, and he sat looking around.
Yuŋkȟáŋ waná wíŋyaŋ k’uŋ hé išnála yaŋká kéčhiŋ na šiná waŋ wičhápȟaha ečé uŋ káǧapi čha glubláye.
And so then the woman thought that she was sitting all alone, and she spread out her blanket made of scalps.
Na leyé, “Lé naháŋȟči waglúštaŋ šni.
And she said, “I still haven’t finished this.
Hó éyaš Hakéla pȟehíŋ šikšíčela kiŋ iwáču kiŋháŋ, heháŋl éwakihuŋni kte ’uŋ!” eyé.
But when I take Hakéla’s really bad hair, then I will have finished!” Hakéla thi-ákaŋl hé naȟ’úŋ yaŋká čha líla nihíŋčiye-la, na čhiyéku kiŋ glípi eháŋl wáŋčag owíčhakiyake.
Hakéla was really scared sitting there listening on the tipi, and when the brothers came back, he told them it immediately.
Akhé híŋhaŋni čhaŋkhé kȟoškalaka kiŋ ománi iyáyapi.
Again it was the morning, and so the young men left walking around.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl wíŋyaŋ k’uŋ wóžuha gluškíŋ na etáŋhaŋ táku eyá gmigmágma čha šiŋšíŋyela púza čha napóžula ičú.
And so then she untied a bag of hers and she took a handful of round and wrinkled dry things from it.
Héčhuŋ na waháŋpi kiŋ él okála na ečhél khičháŋye.
She did that, and she sprinkled them on the soup, and she spiked it like that.
Waná špáŋ čhaŋkhé wótiŋ kta čha waháŋpi eyá kaptá ičú.
Then it was cooked, and so she would drink it, so she ladled up some soup.
Yuŋkȟáŋ leyaš táku ókaȟ hiyéye kiŋ hená wičhánuŋǧe héčha!
But the things that were scattered throughout were human ears!
Hé é čha pusyá yuhá na eyášna yúte.
It was that that she had dry, and she often ate them.
Héčhuŋ kiŋ él Hakéla thi-ákaŋl waŋyáŋg yaŋká čha áta tȟaŋságt’e.
Hakéla sat on the tipi seeing her doing that, so he was scared to death.
Na čhiyéku glípi eháŋl héčhena owíčhakiyake.
And when the brothers came back, he still told them about it.
Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Naúŋpȟapi kta iyéčheča yeló!” eyápi.
And so they said, “we should run away!” Hó éyaš tȟokéya wíkȟaŋ waŋ ičúpi na tónačhaŋ heháŋyaŋ aŋpétu ál’ataya čhéǧa waŋ ogná piȟyápi, wíŋyaŋ kiŋ ománi iyáya čhaŋ.
But they took a rope first, and they boiled it in a kettle for several days all day, when the woman left walking around.
Waná tȟéhaŋ héčhuŋpi na pusyápi čha tókhel okíhika pȟaŋpȟáŋla.
Then they did that for a long time, and they dried it, so it was as fragile as coupld be.
Yuŋkȟáŋ aŋpétu waŋ él, wíŋyaŋ k’uŋ glí čha, “Hó, tȟaŋkší, čhaŋk’íŋ yá yo,” eyápi.
And so one day, the woman came back, so they said, “hey, younger sister, go gather wood.” “Čháŋ čhoǧíŋ šašá hená ečé akú wo,” eyápi.
They said, “come back with the red pith of a tree.” Wíkȟaŋ kiŋ k’úpi na wíŋyaŋ kiŋ čhaŋmáhel iyáye.
They gave her the rope, and the woman went outside.
Pahá aísiŋyaŋ iyáya yuŋkȟáŋ héčhena ȟčiŋ uŋwéya pȟakíȟtapi na líla napȟápi.
She left to the other side of the hill, and so they bundled up her travel provisions, and she left.
Kȟoháŋ, wíŋyaŋ kiŋ čháŋkaksa-he.
In the meantime, she was chopping wood.
Hó éyaš čhoǧíŋ šašá kiŋ hená (čhaŋšúška eyápi héčha) líla suksúta na okákse šíča čha tȟéhaŋ theȟíya škáŋ.
But those piths of the trees were really hard (they were box elders), and they were hard to cut, so she was working hard for a long time.
Kȟoškálaka kiŋ héčhel slolyápi čha hé uŋ čháŋ héčha ečéla akú-šípi.
The young men knew that, so for that reason they told her to only come back with wood like that.
Waná ok’íŋ waŋ káǧe éyaš yuhá inážiŋ kta khéš ečháŋl wíkȟaŋ kiŋ kapsáka na čháŋ kiŋ okálaya hiŋȟpáye.
Then she made a carrying pack, but when she arrived with it, the rope broke and the wood fell scattering about. Čha áta iyótiyekiye.
So it was a really hard process.
Héčhel théȟiya kú naháŋ haŋkéya thiyótȟaŋiŋyaŋ glihúŋni yuŋkȟáŋ waná othíwota.
She came back in a hard way like that, and finally she arrived back with the tipi in sight, and so it was an old campsite. Čha áta íšiŋkčhiŋ heyé, “Waȟtéšni šíčapi kiŋ!
So she said angrily, “pieces of trash!
Eháŋk’uŋ lená ečhákel akú-mašípe lé.
It’s clear that they told me to do that on purpose. Éyaš tókȟa šni.
But that’s no problem.
Owíčhabluspiŋ kte ’uŋ,” eyíŋ na líla wičhákhuwa.
I will catch you,” she said, and she chased after them.
Hakíktakta íŋyaŋkapi uŋkȟáŋ waná wičhílazata pahá waŋ aíyoȟpeya áta makȟá šóta s’e hiyú!
They ran looking behind themselves, and so she came behind them like smoke rolling downhill!
Waná khiyéla ú čha tȟokápȟa kiŋ uŋkčéla waŋ pahí na héktakiya kaȟ’ól iyéye.
Then she was coming close, so the oldest one picked up a cactus, and he threw it behind.
Yuŋkȟáŋ lazáta makȟá kiŋ áta uŋkčékčela hiŋglá čha wíŋyaŋ kiŋ íȟaŋhaŋ tókȟani hiyú šni.
And so the ground behind them suddenly became a cactus bed, so the woman wasn’t able to come for a little while.
Haŋkéya ȟ’aŋhíya, čhaŋkú iwáŋyaŋg okógnagna okáwiŋȟwiŋȟ ú čha kȟoháŋ líla tȟéhaŋ iȟpéya iyáyapi.
Finally, she was coming turning around between the roads looking through, so in the meanwhile they ran away for a long time.
Hó éyaš wíŋyaŋ kiŋ líla kaȟwóka čha íyečhana akhé wičháyuza nuŋs’é aú!
But the woman was really quick, so she came almost catching them shortly thereafter.
Hé uŋ, tȟokápȟa íyokhihe kiŋ hé míla gluslútiŋ na héktakiya iȟpéye.
For that reason, the next oldest one pulled out his knife, and he threw it behind himself.
Yuŋkȟáŋ áta makȟá etáŋhaŋ míla pȟestóstola yawá-phičášniyaŋ óta čha hináphiŋ na ówaŋčaya pȟestóstoya hé.
And so there were countless sharp knives sticking out of the earth, so they came in sight, and they stood up sharp everywhere. Čha akhé hél owátohaŋyaŋ ú okíhi šni.
So she couldn’t come at them for a short time again. Éyaš tóškhe akhé naíč’išpiŋ na wičhákigleǧa nuŋs’é aú!
But she escaped again somehow, and she was coming almost catching up with them!
Wáŋčagna nihíŋčiya makhínaȟtaȟtakapi uŋkȟáŋ makȟá kiŋ áta wičhíhakab glakíŋyaŋ našléče.
Immediately they stomped and stomped out of fear, and so behind them the earth cracked here and there behind them. Čhaŋkhé wíŋyaŋ kiŋ sáŋm hiyú okíhi šni.
And so the woman couldn’t come any more.
Héčhena líla íŋyaŋkapi na wakpá waŋ otȟáŋka čha glakíŋyaŋ ȟpáya yuŋkȟáŋ él inážiŋpi.
They kept sprinting, and they arrived at a large river that was laying across.
Waná čhopȟá iyúweǧapi kta háŋl, ečháŋl winúȟčala tȟókeča waŋ tokhéčela máni-la čha čhaŋyátaŋhaŋ hiyú.
When they were crossing it by wading into it, a different old woman came barely walking from the woods.
Na hé heyé, “Tȟakožá, kȟowákataŋ éiȟpemaya pe,” ewíčhakiye.
And she told them, “grandchildren, bring me across the river.” Líla ináȟnipi na nakúŋ nihíŋčiyapi éyaš akhé úŋšilapi.
They were really rushing, and they were scared too, but they were also compassionate. Čha waŋží k’íŋ na iyúweȟ aí yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl heyé, “Íŋše lé óčhičiyapi kta tkȟá waúŋšiyalapi héčiŋhaŋ slolwáya wačhíŋ ye.
So one of them put her on his shoulders, and crossed with her, and so she said, “I was just going to help you, but I wanted to know if you all were compassionate. Čha waná khiglá pe!
So then go back the way you came!
Wíŋyaŋ kiŋ ečháni ú kte kštó,” eyá čha akhé líla napȟápi.
The woman will be coming here,” she said, so they left again.
Héčhuŋpi na ítohaŋtu yuŋkȟáŋ wíŋyaŋ šíče k’uŋ mnióhuta ektá hihúŋni.
They did that and a little later the bad woman arrived at the edge of the water.
Hél áta blézešniyaŋ glakíŋkiŋyaŋ mni-áglagla oíŋyaŋke.
There she was walking around the edge of the water back and forth all crazed. Čhaŋkhé winúȟčala k’uŋ wíŋyeya nážiŋ.
And so the old woman stood ready.
Wíŋyaŋ šíče k’uŋ mní él čhophíŋ kte ȟčeháŋl winúȟčala kiŋ pȟéta waŋ wakpá él oíȟpeye.
Right when the bad woman was about to wade into the water, the old woman threw a torch into the water. Čha áta iléšaša hiŋgníŋ na mní k’uŋ áta pȟéta hiŋglé.
So suddenly red flames burst up, and all of the water became fire. Čhaŋkhé hetáŋhaŋ kȟoškálaka kiŋ kpaptápi na thiyáta ní khípi škhé.
And so the young men survived from there, and they got back home alive.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

12 – Yašlé é na Matȟó Yašlé waŋ tókhiya yá-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ othíweta waŋ él míla waŋ iyéyiŋ na yuhá héčhena yé.
A coyote went going somewhere when he found a knife in an abandoned campsite and he still went along with it. Ȟčeháŋl pahá akȟótaŋhaŋ matȟó tȟáŋka waŋ ú čha kȟokípȟa čhaŋkhé blokáskaska-ič’íye.
Just then a big bear came from the other side of a hill so he was scared and so he was hiccupping himself.
Héčhuŋ na heyé, “Wáŋ, híŋhaŋni kiŋ lé táku tȟáŋka tȟebwáya owákihi čha léčhel blowákaska-he!” eyé.
He did that and he said, “hey, this morning I could gobble down something big so that’s why I’m hiccuping.” Yuŋkȟáŋ matȟó kiŋ íŋš-eyá blokáskaska na, “Wáŋ, híŋhaŋni kiŋ lé táku níškola tȟebwáya owákihi čha léčhel blowákaska-he k’uŋ!” eyé.
And so the bear hiccuped too and he said, “hey, this morning I could gobble down something as small as this so you know that’s why I’m hiccuping!” Heyá čha heháŋl yašlé k’uŋ íčat’a kȟópȟegla čha míla waŋ iyéye uŋ hé pazózo.
He said that so then the Coyote was extremely afraid so he showed off the knife that he had found.
Na nakúŋ olówaŋ waŋ léčhuŋp-s’e hópȟestoya yawáŋkal éyaye: “Tuwá mitȟókab hiyáya čháŋšna čhawápȟapȟa na wakté yeló.” And he also raised his voice in song in a squeaky way and said, “whenever someone passes in front of me I stab and stab away and kill them.” Yuŋkȟáŋ Matȟó íŋš waŋží ahíyayiŋ na; “Tuwá mitȟókab hiyáya čháŋšna blaȟúȟuǧiŋ na wakté yeló,” eyá lowáŋ.
So Bear also sang one and he sang, “whenever someone passes in front of me, I chew them to pieces and kill them.” Hó éyaš Yašlé naȟ’úŋ šni kúŋs akíš’aš’a na míla k’uŋ yuptáŋptaŋ na matȟó uŋ khuwá éyaye.
But Coyote, pretending not to hear, gave off some war whoops and turned the knife over and over and he chased the bear. Čha iyé tȟáŋka k’éyaš nakípȟiŋ na čhaŋóžu waŋ él khinážiŋ.
So he was big himself but he ran away and he stopped in a forest.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé akhé, “Mačík’ala éyaš tuwá čhaŋwóhaŋ ináȟma čháŋšna pazáŋzaŋ él mníŋ na čhawápȟapȟa ’uŋ,” eyá lowáŋ.
And so Coyote sang again, “I’m small but whenever someone hides among the trees, I go looking through it and stab away.” Heyá yuŋkȟáŋ Matȟó akhé napȟíŋ na wakpá waŋ él mničhókab inážiŋ.
He said that and so Bear ran away again and he stopped in the middle of the water in a river.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé, “Tuwá mničhókaya onápȟa čháŋšna mní él kignúŋg iblámniŋ na čhawápȟapȟa ’uŋ!” eyá lowáŋ.
And so Coyote sang, “Whenever someone flees into the middle of the water, I dive into the water and I stab away!” Hó čha Matȟó akhé napȟá uŋkȟáŋ waná heháŋyaŋ Yašlé kȟokípȟe šni.
So Bear ran away again and so then Coyote wasn’t scared anymore. Čhaŋkhé Yašlé heyé, “Wáŋ, Misúŋ, kú wo.
And so Coyote said, “hey, little brother, c’mon back.
Wačhíŋka kiŋháŋ eháŋni čhačhípȟapȟa kta kȟá.
If I had wanted to, I would have already stabbed you. Íŋše lé waškátiŋ na hepȟá-he ló!” eyá čha Matȟó él glí.
I was just playing and that’s all I’m saying,” he said so Bear came to him.
Héčheš hetáŋhaŋ zuyá yápi yuŋkȟáŋ maštíŋčala waŋ čhaŋkú glakíŋyaŋ hiyáye.
So from there they went on the warpath and so a rabbit was crossing the road.
Héčhena Yašlé: “Wáŋ, Maštíŋčala; nakpá háŋskaska na phuté ȟčiȟčí!
Hiyú uŋtȟó.
Coyote said, “Hey, rabbit; long ears and ragged nose!
C’mon.
Zuyá uŋyáŋpi čha níš-eyá óyapȟa kte ló,” eyé.
We’re on the warpath so you should join in, too.” Yuŋkȟáŋ Maštíŋčala íŋš, “Éyaš oímayaȟaȟa k’uŋ,” eyé.
And so rabbit said himself, “but you made fun of me.” Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé akhé, “Huŋhuŋhí, íŋše ówehaŋhaŋ ‘Pȟežíȟóta mnála’ epȟé ’uŋ.
And so Coyote said again, “Goodness, I was just saying ‘it smells like white sage’ as a joke.
Tasé oštéšteya waéčhičiye ka!” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ ognáye wašté čha wičála na ób iyáye.
Of course I didn’t say those weird things to you!” he said, and so he tricked him good so he believed him and he went with them.
Waná wí khúčiyela yaŋká čha patȟág inážiŋpi na pȟeží wókheya waŋ káǧapi.
Then the sun was low so they stopped there and they made a grass shelter.
Thimá íyotakapi ȟčeháŋl waná tȟóka eyá ahíhuŋni na áta aóglutȟewičhayapi.
Right when they sat down inside, some enemies arrived and they completely surrounded them.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Matȟó thiyópa ot’íŋš yaŋká čha Yašlé heyé, “Wáŋ, Misúŋ, makíyukȟaŋ yo!
And so Bear sat in the doorway so Coyote said, “hey, little brother, make room for me!
Miyéš wičháwakhute wačhíŋ ’uŋ,” eyé.
I want to be the one to shoot them.” Heyé k’éyaš tȟóka kiŋ uŋgnáhelaka kakhíyotȟaŋ eyáye.
He said that but suddenly the enemies left in some direction. Čha hétkiya wičhákhuwa íŋyaŋkapi na pahá aísiŋyaŋ khíyotakapi.
So they ran following them in that direction and they sat down on the other side of a hill.
Yuŋkȟáŋ tȟóka kiŋ akhé aglíhuŋni na ób kičhízapi.
And so the enemies arrived back and they fought with them.
Owátohaŋyaŋ héčhuŋpi na óhaŋketa napȟéwičhayapi čha tȟóka k’uŋ áta ábeya akhíyagle.
They did that for a short time and finally they drove them off so all the enemies scattered off from where they came from in different directions. Éyaš itȟókab iyé líla wičháopi.
But beforehand they shot at them a bunch.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé heyé, “Itȟó, líla uŋkópi čha waná mázasu kiŋ ihóȟpa hiyúuŋkiyapi kte ló.
And so Coyote said, “I guess, they really shot us so now we should shoot out our bullets.
Tuwé núm ótaȟča hiyúuŋyaŋpi héči henáos ohíyapi kte ’uŋ.
If two of us shoots a whole lot, those two will win.
Na waŋží íŋš čónala hiyúye kiŋ hé uŋmá kiŋ ktépi na yútapi kte ló!” eyé.
And the one who shot the fewest bullets will be killed and eaten!” Yuŋkȟáŋ héčhetulapi čhaŋkhé waná mázasu kašpášpa hiyúkiyapi.
And so they agreed and so they spat away with their bullets.
Matȟó líla óta akhígle ópi čha iyé mázasu ótaȟča hiyúye.
Bear shot just a whole lot of times so he shot out a lot of bullets.
Hó éyaš ištógmus yaŋkíŋ na héčhuŋ čha kȟoháŋ Yašlé Maštíŋčala kičhí ókšaŋtaŋhaŋ amánuŋpi.
But he sat with his eyes closed and he did that so meanwhile Coyote stole them from all around him with Rabbit.
Waná henála yuŋkȟáŋ Matȟó itȟáŋokšaŋ čónala yuhá (amánuŋpi kiŋ uŋ) čha ktépi kta kéyapi yuŋkȟáŋ héčhetukila čhaŋkhé ktépi.
Now there was nothing left and so Bear had few around him (them having stole them) so they said that they would kill him and so he agreed and so they killed him.
Hó éyaš yútapi šni.
But they didn’t eat him.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé Maštíŋčala hekíye, “Hó waná wakȟáŋyaŋ wówahiŋ kta čha iyáyiŋ na Uŋčí tȟa-čhéǧa k’uŋ makáku wo,” eyé.
And so Coyote said to Rabbit, “Well now I will cook some sacred things so go and bring me back grandma’s kettle.” Čha Maštíŋčala psípsil pahá aínab iyáyiŋ na áškaŋ šni s’e čhéǧa waŋ yuphíya wiyákpa čha aglí.
So Rabbit left jumping over to the other side of the hill and he came back quickly with a kettle that was well polished.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé hé é šni kéyiŋ na akhé hiyóye ší na heháŋl čhéǧa waŋ šá líla wašté aglí.
And so Coyote said that that wasn’t it and he sent him there to get it again and then he came back with a really nice red kettle.
Akhé hé é šni kéyiŋ na tȟókeča waŋ akú ší čhaŋkhé waŋží tȟoyéla wašté čha aglí.
Again, he told him that that wasn’t it and he send him back again to get a different one and so he came back with a nice blue one.
Héčhuŋ k’éyaš akhé hé é šni kéya čhaŋkhé akhé iyáye.
He did that but again he said that that wasn’t it and so he left again.
Yuŋkȟáŋ čhéǧa waŋ áta šilyéla ȟuȟúǧahaŋ čha aglí.
And so he came back with a pot that was all banged up and red.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé, “Wáŋ, wačhíŋtȟuŋšni-la!
And so Coyote said, “hey, stupid!
Nakéš hé é yeló.
Finally this is it. Áta…eháš iníč’iȟaȟa yeló!” eyé.
What a fool you always make of yourself.” Waná tȟaló eyá iyóȟpeyiŋ na Maštíŋčala wayáotȟaŋiŋ ší.
Then he boiled some meat and he told Rabbit to make an announcement about it. Čha éyapaha ománi yuŋkȟáŋ enánataŋhaŋ wamákȟaškaŋ kiŋ iyúha, matȟápiȟ’a yéš kȟó ahíhuŋni.
So he walked around announcing it and so from back in those place all the animals, even including toad, came to there.
Hípi na íŋyaŋ blaská waŋ akáŋl Yašlé itȟáŋčhaŋ égnakapi čha waná wičháša tȟáŋka ič’íla.
They came and they sat Coyote on top of a flat rock like a chief so then he saw himself as an important man.
Ištógmus, ǧúŋǧa yaŋká-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ ečháŋl wamákȟaškaŋ k’uŋ táku iȟ’áŋ-he uŋ hená, pápa kȟóya, áta ikhíničapi.
With his hand of his mouth, he was sitting haughtily and so just then the animals all fought over the things that he had been cooking, including the jerky.
Hó čha khuwá éwičhayayiŋ kte éyaš íŋyaŋ k’uŋ éna uŋzé kiŋ áta iyáskape!
So he was going to chase them off but his butt was all stuck to the rock back there. Čha líla páŋ éyaš ečháŋl ábebekiya khiglápi.
So he screamed out but just then they left to return where they came from all scattered. Áta “Pápa mič’íčhaǧe ’uŋ!” eyáya čhéya úŋ.
He was crying out, “you know that I’m made that jerky for myself!” Yuŋkȟáŋ kȟoháŋ uŋkčékhiȟa waŋ pápa k’uŋ yuhá kiŋyáŋ hiyáya čha waŋglákiŋ na iyótaŋ líla čhéya-he.
And so meanwhile a magpie flew by with the jerky so he saw it and he cried really bad.
Ičhúŋhaŋ, píško eyá itȟáŋokšaŋ okíŋyaŋpi čha heyá páŋ; “Misúŋ, misúŋ, theȟíya maŋké ló!
In the meantime, some nighthawks flew around him so he yelled out, “Little brother, little brother, I’m doin’ bad! Úŋšimala po!” eyé.
Have pity on me!” Hó éyaš él étuŋwe šni okíŋyaŋ-haŋpi yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl, “Ničhíŋča kiŋ wičhábluonihaŋ kte ló!” eyá wičhákipaŋ.
But they flew around not paying attention to him and so then he yelled out to them, “I will be respectful to your children!” Yuŋkȟáŋ píško uŋ waŋží khúčiyela kiŋyáŋ hiyú na kawáŋkal khiglé.
And so a nighthawk came soaring down and turned back up.
Ičhúŋhaŋ líla úŋkče hiŋglé kač’úŋ Yašlé kákhi tókhiya áta kaȟláya iȟpéye!
Meanwhile, because he farted all of a sudden, Coyote was peeled off of the rock thrown over there in some direction.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Yašlé heyé, “É, šilyéla!
And so Coyote said, “hey, ugly! Íŋše ikčéya maŋká-he k’uŋ!
You know I was just sitting here.
Tuwáš heháŋyaŋ ohítiya hokší-thekíȟila ka,” eyíŋ na nážiŋ hiyáye.
He’s someone who really must love his children,” he said and got up.
Wáŋčag uŋkčékhiȟa waŋ pápa kiŋ khí k’uŋ hé iyátȟab yíŋ na mní waŋ él ihúŋni.
Immediately he went following the magpie that stole the jerky and he arrived at a body of water.
Mni-máhel étuŋwaŋ yuŋkȟáŋ héčhiya uŋkčékhiȟa uŋ hé yaŋkíŋ na pápa waŋ amánuŋ k’uŋ hé ahípazo s’eléčheča.
He looked in the water and so there he saw that magpie sat down and it was like he was holding out the meat that he stole.
Hó čha Yašlé líla čhaŋzékiŋ na kignúŋg iyáye.
So Coyote was really angry and dove in.
Wakpála čheté él yutȟáŋtȟaŋ olé k’éyaš oníya ptéčela.
He searched feeling around on the bottom of the river but he didn’t have enough breath. Čha héčhetu khéš óhaŋketa nawáŋkal khiglé.
But whenever he was like that, finally he came back up. Šeháŋštukȟa waná íŋyaŋ eyá ičú na pȟaǧé él aígluskitiŋ na akhé kignúg iyáye.
For sure this time he took some rocks and binded them around himself and he dove in again.
Hó éyaš tké kiŋ uŋ áta okáspiŋ na khutákiya iyáye!
But he sank on account of his weight and he went downward!
Yuŋkȟáŋ niyášni t’íŋ kta wahéhaŋl tokhéčela kpaptíŋ na puzáta khihúŋni.
And so at about the time that he was about to die from lack of breath, he barely survived and he returned back to dry land.
Yuŋkȟáŋ leyáš halháta k’uŋ čhaŋ-wáŋkal yaŋkíŋ na pápa kiŋ kiyúta-he.
And so all the while the magpie sat on a branch and he ate his jerky.
Hó éyaš mní kiŋ míyoglas’iŋ s’e ȟpáyiŋ na hé Yašlé gnáye, éyaš abléze šni.
But the water was like a mirror and it tricked Coyote, but he didn’t realize that.
Waná waštéyela tȟebkhíyiŋ na iyáyiŋ kta háŋl Yašlé ihúkhul kašé.
Now he was completely eating his foot and just when he left, Coyote tripped over.
Kaítuŋkab gliȟpáyiŋ na waŋkál étuŋwaŋ ȟpáya yuŋkȟáŋ nakéš waŋyáŋka škhé.
He fell down on his back and he lay looking upwards and so finally he saw him.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.
13 – Khéya Zuyá Yé Aŋpétu waŋ él, Khéya ozúye olé ománi.
One day, Snapping Turtle was walking around looking for a war party.
Yuŋkȟáŋ pȟatkáša waŋ él hí na “Čhiyé, tókhiya lá hwo?” eyá čha, “Íŋše zuyá blé,” eyá ayúpte.
And so he came up to a turtle and he said, “little brother, where are you going?” so he responded, “I’m just going on the warpath.” Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Itȟó, Čhiyé, uŋyíŋ kte ló!” eyá čha kičhí yé.
And so he said, “I guess I’ll go with you,” and he went with him.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Čhaȟóta hiyáyiŋ na waŋwíčhayaŋka čha inážiŋ.
And so Ashes went past and he saw them so he stopped there. “Tókhiya lápi hwo?” eyá čha okíyakapi. “Where are you two going?” he said so they told him. Čha ób yíŋ kta kéye.
So it is said that he went with them.
Waná yámni yá-haŋpi, yuŋkȟáŋ zičá waŋ íŋš-eyá ópȟa kta kéye.
Then the three of them went along, and so it is said that a squirrel joined them, too. Čha waná tópa zuyá yápi.
So the four of them went on the warpath. Íyokhiheya waléǧa waŋ é na thuswéčha waŋ ahí-ópȟapi čha waná šákpepi.
Next a bladder and a dragonfly came and joined them so then there were six of them. Čha áta iyókiphiya lowáŋwaŋ yápi.
So they all went happily along singing.
Tȟóka wičhóthi kiŋ ihúŋnipi šni haŋní, wakpála waŋ iyúweǧapi.
Before they arrived reached the enemy camp, they crossed a river.
Líla ȟliȟlíla čha Pȟatkáša kaȟlí na glinápȟa okíhi šni čha iȟpéya iyáyapi.
It was really muddy so turtle got stuck and he couldn’t get back up so they left him behind.
Héčheš záptaŋla waná yápi.
And so now just five of them went along.
Yuŋkȟáŋ útahučhaŋ tȟáŋka waŋ oȟláthe iyáyapi yuŋkȟáŋ hél úta líla óta enána hiyéye.
And so they went under a big red oak tree and so there were many acorns scattered about back there.
Wáŋčagna Zičá sáŋpȟa yá čhíŋ šni kéya čha úŋyaŋ iyáyapi.
It’s said that squirrel immediately didn’t want to go further so they left him behind.
Tóblala waná zuyá yápi yuŋkȟáŋ uŋgnáhela tȟaté hiyú, na Čhaȟóta woblú iyéye.
Then just four of them went along and so suddenly the wind blew, and Ashes was blown away.
Heháŋl wóȟešma waŋ ópta yápi naháŋ el wapȟépȟeka óta, čha Waléǧa iyúȟlečiŋ na sníze!
Then they went through a patch of thick grass and there were a lot of brambles there, so Bladder tore against them and he deflated!
Hó, waná Thuswéčha Khéya kičhíla iháŋpi.
Then Dragonfly ran into Snapping Turtle.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Thuswéčha uŋgnáhela pšá na čhaŋkȟáhu kiŋ katȟáŋkal iyékiya čha t’é.
And so Dragonfly suddenly sneezed and he blew out his back so he died.
Yuŋkȟáŋ Khéya heíč’iye; “É, iyéš mišnála waú kiŋ wašté kȟá,” eyíŋ na waná tȟóka thípi kiŋ ektá ihúŋni na él iyáye.
And so Snapping Turtle said to himself, “Huh, it should be good that I’m the one to be coming here all alone,” and then he reached the enemy tipis and he went there.
Yuŋkȟáŋ oyáte kiŋ, “Wáŋ, táku héčhiyataŋhaŋ ú weló,” eyápi na áta ok’óke.
And so the people said, “Hey, something is coming from over there,” and there was a fuss.
Ičúpi na wíŋyaŋ kiŋ iyótaŋ waštélakapi na, “Má! Čhéwiŋš waštéla ké!
Tákula he?” eyá nážiŋpi.
They took him and the women especially liked him, and they stood saying, “Wow!
How cute is he!
What is he?” Tákuwe hí kiŋ iyúŋǧapi čha, “Zuyá wahí yeló!” eyá yuŋkȟáŋ, “Huŋhí! Čísčila éyaš zuyá hí-la kštó,” eyápi, wíŋyaŋ kiŋ iyúha.
They asked him why he came so he said, “I came on the warpath!” and so all the women said, “Gosh!
He’s small but he just came on the warpath.” Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Zuyá hí kiŋ lé táku kȟokípȟe-la sél?” eyápi.
And so they said, “I wonder what this thing that came on the warpath is afraid of?” Yuŋkȟáŋ, “Pȟéta kȟoyákipȟa he?” eyápi uŋkȟáŋ, “Hóȟ, hé átaš wókȟokpȟeke šni!” eyé.
And so they said, “are you afraid of fire?” and so he said, “Oh absolutely not, I’m not afraid of it at all!” Čha heháŋl táku wókȟokpȟeke kiŋ iyúha čhažéyatapi éyaš hená átaš ítokȟa šni kéye.
So then they named all the sorts of things that are dangerous but it’s said that he didn’t worry about any of them.
(Iyúha itéšniyaŋ kȟokípȟe éyaš wičhágnaye wačhíŋ.)
(He was very much afraid of all of them but he was trying to trick them.) Óhaŋketa mni-máhel iȟpéyapi kta kéyapi uŋkȟáŋ ité agláȟpiŋ na čhéye.
Finally they said that they would throw him into the water and so he covered his face and cried. “Mní ečéla kȟowákpȟe!” eyíŋ na líla čhéya čha wakpá-ta áyapi na čhokáta oíȟpeyapi. “I’m only afraid of the water!” he said and he was really crying so they brought him to the river and they threw him into the middle of it. Íŋyaŋ s’e mahél iyáya čha khiglápi kta uŋkȟáŋ ȟčeháŋl glinápȟiŋ na áta akíš’aš’a nuŋwáŋ hiyáye.
He went in like a rock so just when he was about to return back from where he came, he came back into sight and he swam along giving some war whoops.
Héčhuŋ na iyé khiglé.
He did that and he himself went back to where he came from.
Thiyáta khihúŋni yuŋkȟáŋ khéya oyáte kiŋ áta wóiyuškiŋ uŋ wačhípi škhé.
He reached back home and so snapping turtle nation danced with joy.
Leháŋl tuwá táku waŋží líla čhíŋ éyaš wičála šni kúŋza čháŋšna, Lakȟóta kiŋ leyápi: “Khéya čha mní él áyapi kte ȟčiŋ s’e” eyápi.
Nowadays whenever someone really wants something but they pretend not to believe that, Lakota people say, “they’re a turtle so it’s like they really want to be brought to the water.” Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

14 – Tȟašíyagnuŋpa É na Siŋtéȟla Kiŋ Pȟeží háŋskaska ožú waŋ él, tȟašíyagnuŋpa wíŋyela waŋ hoȟpí kíčhaǧe.
In a growth of long grass, a female meadowlark made her nest.
Yuŋkȟáŋ waná čhiŋčá kiŋ tȟaŋkíŋkiŋyaŋ áyapi éyaš naháŋȟči kiŋyáŋpi uŋspépi-la šni.
And so then she was carrying her large children but they still didn’t know how to fly.
Aŋpétu waŋ él wítaya yaŋkápi ȟčeháŋl uŋgnáhela siŋtéȟla waŋ wahóȟpi kiŋ ohómni hiyúŋke!
When one day they were sitting together suddenly a rattlesnake came and lied down around the nest.
Líla tȟašíyagnuŋpa húŋkupi kiŋ kȟokípȟa čha áta čhaŋtíyapȟa.
The meadowlarks’ mom was very scared so her heart was beating very hard.
Hó éyaš ítokȟa šni s’e iyókiphi kúŋziŋ na heyé, “Huŋhí, Čhiŋkší, nilékši kiŋ waná hí čha lol’íwečiȟ’aŋ kte.
But, as if she wasn’t worried, she pretended to be happy and she said, “well, my son, your uncle just came here so I will cook for him.
Iyáyiŋ na čhéǧa waŋží olé ye!” eyé.
Leave and look for a kettle!” Čha héčhena čhiŋčá tȟokápȟa kiŋ kiŋyáŋ iyáye.
So then the oldest one left flying.
Hé tȟoká kiŋyé wačhíŋ-la éyaš taŋyáŋkel iyáye.
That one just tried to fly for the first time but he left pleasently.
Tȟéhaŋ glí šni yuŋkȟáŋ tȟašíyagnuŋpa wíŋyela k’uŋ heyé, “Huŋhí, čhéǧa olé iyáye uŋ hé tȟéhaŋ glí-la šni!” He didn’t come back after a long while and so the female meadowlark said, “well, that one who went to look for a kettle just hasn’t been back for a while!” Heyíŋ na čhiŋčá íyokhihe kiŋ čhiyéku okíle yeší.
She said that and she told her next oldest son to go look for his older brother. Čha hé íŋš-eyá iyáyiŋ na akhé tókhi iyáya tȟaŋíŋ šni.
So that one left too and again he left somewhere until he wasn’t visible anymore.
Akhé waŋží yeší čha iyáya yuŋkȟáŋ čík’ala ȟče kiŋ waná ečéla éna yaŋké.
She told one to leave again and so he left and so then only the littlest one was sat back there.
Waná siŋtéȟla k’uŋ apȟé kapíŋ na phiphíya iyúŋka čha ziŋtkála kiŋ áta nihíŋčiye.
Then the rattlesnake got tired of waiting and he lied down again and again so the bird was really scared.
Yuŋkȟáŋ heyé, “Hakéla, Čhíŋkš, ničhíye awíčhatuŋwe yá ye!
And so she said, “Littlest one, my son, go look for your older brothers!
Uŋgnáš núnipi séče,” eyá čha íŋš-eyá kiŋyáŋ iyáye-la.
Maybe they got lost,” so he just left flying away, too.
Héčhel iyúha iglúsotapi na heháŋl tȟašíyagnuŋpa wíŋyela k’uŋ ákablablaǧiŋ na, “Hó, hél ȟpáya ye.
In that way they were all gone and then the female meadowlark spread her wings and said, “well, keep laying down here.” Tuwé čha lol’íničiȟ’aŋ kta héči!” eyíŋ na íŋš-eyá tokhíyotȟaŋ iyáye.
I wonder who it is that will make your food!” and she left to somewhere, too.
Yuŋkȟáŋ zuzéča kiŋ gnáyaŋpi čha akíbleza škhé.
And so the snake realized that they tricked him.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
The end.

15 – Hokšíčhaŋlkiyapi Tȟa-šína Kiŋ Kȟoškálaka waŋ líla theȟílapi yuŋkȟáŋ hé šiná waŋ waštéȟča čha yuhá.
A boy was very loved and so it was a really nice blanket that he had. Óhiŋniyaŋ ahókipȟiŋ na nakúŋ glušlóka čháŋna waŋkál otkékiye s’a.
He took good care of it and also whenever he took it off, he would always hang it up.
Yuŋkȟáŋ haŋhépi waŋ él, tuwá manúŋ éyaye.
And so at night, someone went away having stolen it. Čha ihíŋhaŋni yuŋkȟáŋ tákuni otké šni kiŋ waŋyáŋkiŋ na líla ičháŋtešiče.
So the next morning, he saw that nothing was hanging and he was very sad about that.
Wáŋčag okíle iyáyiŋ na yá-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ uŋgná igmú tȟáŋka waŋ khušéya hinážiŋ čha atáye.
Immediately he went to look for it and was going and so suddenly a cougar stopped blocking the path so he ran into him.
Yuŋkȟáŋ hé hekíye, “Tókhiya lá hwo, Tȟakóža?
And so he said, “Where are you going, Grandchild? Šiná kiŋ oyákile héči, lél ogná tuwá yuhá yá čha waŋbláke.
If you lost your blanket, I saw someone who was going along with it along this way. Čha héčhena okíle yo, Tȟakóža.
So still look for it, Grandchild.
Hó éyaš, táku tókȟa háŋtaŋš ečháš anámataŋ yo!” ekíye.
But, if something goes wrong, be sure to run towards me,” he told him. Čha hetáŋhaŋ yá yuŋkȟáŋ heháŋl ziŋtkála waŋ ptehíŋčičila eyápi héčha čha čhaŋwáŋkal yaŋká uŋkȟáŋ atáye.
So he was going from there and so then he saw a type of bird called a killdeer so he sat up in a tree and so he ran across him.
Watȟéšlaka sápa waŋ pȟóštaŋ na akíš’aš’a nážiŋ.
He had a black band around his head and he stood there crying out.
Kȟoškálaka k’uŋ waŋyáŋke eháŋl ayáštaŋ na heyé, “Tȟakožá, šiná oyákile načhéča čha táku tókȟa kiŋháŋ anámataŋ yo!” eyé.
When he saw the young man he stopped crying out and he said, “Grandchild, you might be looking for a blanket so if something goes wrong run towards me.” Akhé tȟatȟáŋka waŋ él í yuŋkȟáŋ íŋš-eyá heyé, “Tȟakóža, táku tókȟa kiŋ anámataŋ yo!” ekíye.
Again he got to a buffalo and so he said too, “Grandchild, if something goes wrong, run towards me.” Héčheš yá-haŋ yuŋkȟáŋ čhaŋmáhel thípi waŋ háŋ čha ektá í.
So it was thus that he was going along and so he reached a tipi standing in the woods.
Itȟáŋokšaŋ makȟá kiŋ sutá čha él wakȟáŋyaŋ čhaŋhúta ič’íčhaǧe.
All around the ground was hard so he magically turned into a tree stump.
Héčhel nážiŋ yuŋkȟáŋ wikȟóškalaka waŋ tȟa-šína k’uŋ hé úŋ čha hinápȟiŋ na tȟéhaŋ ayúta nážiŋ.
He stood like that and so a young woman wearing that blanket of his came out and she stood looking at him for a long time. “Tóhaŋni ká čhaŋhúte kiŋ kál hé šni ’uŋ,” eíč’iyiŋ na thimá khiglé. “That stump has surely never stood there before,” she said to herself and she went back inside.
Wáŋčagna akhé kȟoškálaka k’uŋ kiwíčhaša na ektáwapȟaya iyáye.
That young man immediately turned himself back into a person and he went a little further.
Waná čhaŋókpaŋ puŋpúŋ ič’íčhaǧiŋ na thi-kȟáŋyela yuŋké.
Then he turned himself into rotten woodchips and he lay close to the tipi.
Wikȟóškalaka uŋ akhé hinápȟiŋ na tȟéhaŋ ayúl nážiŋ na heháŋl akȟóketkiya yé.
The young woman came out again and for a long time she stood there looking at him and then she went into the distance. Ȟčeháŋl akhé kiwíčhaša hiŋgníŋ na nážiŋ hiyáyiŋ na ilázata hiyú na šiná uŋ hé khi-wáčhiŋ.
Just then he suddenly turned back into a person and he came up behind her and he tried to take the blanket from her.
Wikȟóškalaka kiŋ áta blaǧáhe s’e howáye éyaš kȟoškálaka kiŋ yušlóg khí na gluhá thiyátakiya napȟé!
The young woman let out a howl but the young man pulled it off and he fled back home with it.
Yuŋkȟáŋ wíŋyaŋ kiŋ tȟatȟáŋka gnaškíŋyaŋ ič’íčhaǧiŋ na áta khuwá ahíyu.
And so the woman turned into the Crazy Buffalo and started to chase after him.
Waná ahíkigleǧiŋ kte s’eléčheča uŋkȟáŋ wana ptehíŋčičila uŋ hé anátaŋ na, “Tȟuŋkášila, táku waŋ makté kte ló!” eyé.
Then it seemed like she was about to catch up with him and so then the killdeer charged forward and he said, “Grandfather, something’s going to kill me!” Čhaŋkhé ptehíŋčičila k’uŋ glakíŋkiŋyaŋ khušéya kiŋyé.
And so the killdeer flew back and forth blocking the way.
Héčhuŋ čha tȟatȟáŋka k’uŋ inážiŋ na kičhí kičhíziŋ na ičhúŋhaŋ sémni k’uŋ líla napȟé.
He did that so the buffalo stopped and he fought with it and meanwhile the young man ran away.
Yuŋkȟáŋ tȟatȟáŋg-gnaškíŋyaŋ kiŋ ptehíŋčičila kiŋ tóškhe ktéla naíŋš gnáye lakȟa akhé khuwá-he!
And so the Crazy Buffalo just killed the killdeer somehow or it must have tricked him due to him chasing him again. Čha áta nihíŋčiya íŋyaŋkiŋ naháŋ igmú tȟáŋka uŋ hé tókhiyataŋhaŋ khušéya hinážiŋ.
So he was running really scared and the cougar came up out from somewhere and blocked the path. Čha oȟláthe iyáyiŋ na, “Tȟuŋkášila, táku waŋ makté kte ló!” eyé.
So he went under him and he said, “Grandfather, something’s going to kill me!” Čha íŋš heháŋl waná tȟéhaŋ tȟatȟáŋg-gnaškíŋyaŋ kičhí kičhíze.
So then he himself then fought with the Crazy Buffalo for a long time.
Tȟéhaŋ yuŋkȟáŋ waná igmú tȟáŋka kiŋ hetáŋhaŋ iglášpe lakȟa akhé kigléǧiŋ kta uŋkȟáŋ ȟčeháŋl tȟatȟáŋka ȟčalá k’uŋ hé khušéya hinážiŋ.
It went like that for a long time and so then he must have broken loose from the cougar overtaking him and so just then the old buffalo stood in his path. Čha kȟoškálaka kiŋ áta kaslóhaŋ gliȟpáyiŋ na niyášnišni, “Tȟuŋkášila, táku waŋ makté kte ló!” eyé.
So the young man fell headlong into him and out of breath he said, “Grandfather, something’s going to kill me!” Čha glóglo nážiŋ na waná tȟatȟáŋg-gnaškíŋyaŋ k’uŋ hihúŋni kte.
So he stood grunting and then the Crazy Buffalo was about to reach that place.
Našlóg itkób iyáyiŋ na áta tȟatȟáŋg-gnaškíŋye k’uŋ thezí woȟlél iyéyiŋ na ečhel kté!
He rushed forward facing him and he tore open the Crazy Buffalo’s stomach and he killed him like that!
Hó čha tȟatȟáŋka waŋ ókiye k’uŋ táku wóyuha waštéšte k’ú, na hetáŋhaŋ oyáte thípi ektá khihúŋni.
So he gave the buffalo that helped him some good possessions, and from there he arrived back at his home.
Yuŋkȟáŋ tȟéhaŋ tókȟaȟ’aŋ úŋ čha húŋku na atkúku kiŋ nuphíŋ tȟéhaŋ čhéyapi kač’úŋ ištáǧuŋǧapi.
And so he had disappeared for a long time so, due to both his mother and father crying for a long time, they were blind.
Na nakúŋ wašíglapi uŋ pȟehíŋ glašlápi na úŋšiye ȟčiŋ čhaŋtéšilya yaŋkápi háŋl él wičhágli.
And they also cut their hair in morning his death, and they sat miserably and sadly when he came back to them. Čha maǧážu mní eyá ičú naháŋ ištá kiŋ awíčhakaštaŋ na uŋ ištá wičhákičiyužaža.
So he took some rain water and he poured it into their eyes and using it he washed their eyes for them.
Heháŋl tuŋwáŋpi na wawáŋyaŋkapi.
Then they opened their eyes and they could see.
Nakúŋ natá kiŋ ál’ataya slawíčhayiŋ na pȟehíŋ kiŋ kaháŋskeya wičhákičičasto na uŋ pȟehíŋ háŋskaska uyéwičhakhiye.
He also rubbed all over their heads with annointing oil and he combed their hair smooth with long strokes and in that way he grew their hair for them.
Na heháŋl eháŋni s’e pȟehíŋ kiŋ háŋskeskeya wičhákičisuŋ na ištá kȟó aphíwičhakičíčhiya čha líla philáwičhaya škhé.
And then he braided their hair long for them like in the old days and he also healed their eyes for them and so they thanked him.
Heháŋyela oíhaŋke.
That is all.

16 – Íŋyaŋ Hokšíla (1) Kȟoškálaka tób ówaŋžila thípi yuŋkȟáŋ tȟéča ȟče kiŋ, Hakéla ečíyapi kiŋ hé, čháŋ etáŋ ičú kta inápȟe.
Four young men lived together and so the youngest one, his name was Hakéla, he went outside to gather some wood.
Wáŋčag akhé thimá gličú na wíŋyaŋ waŋ tȟaŋkál nážiŋ-haŋ kéye.
Right away he came back inside and he said that a woman was standing outside. Čhiyéku kiŋ heyá iwíčhayuŋǧe, “Takúuŋyaŋpi kta hwo?” eyá čha, “Tó, tȟaŋkéuŋyaŋpi kte ló.
He asked his older brothers, “how is she related to us?” so they said, “Yeah, she must be our older sister.” Thimáhel kičhó wo,” eyápi.
Invite her inside.” Čha Hakéla khináphiŋ na, “Tȟaŋké, thimá hiyú wo,” eyé.
So Hakéla went back outside and said, “older sister, c’mon inside.” Taŋyáŋ kičhó éyaš ayúpte šni iníla ȟčiŋ thimáhel hiyú na hetáŋhaŋ ób thí.
He invited her inside well but she came inside all quiet not responding and from then on she lived with them.
Waná kȟoškálaka kiŋ ománi yápi kta čha, “Hakéla, awáŋyaŋka yo,” eyápi na iyáyapi.
Then the young men would go walking around so they said, “Hakéla, take care of her,” and they left.
Hakéla héčhena ziŋtkála waŋ škíbibila eyápi héčha ič’íčhaǧiŋ na thičhé íŋkpa ektá kiŋyáŋ iyáhiŋ na él yaŋké.
Hakéla all of a sudden turned into a type of bird that’s called chickadee and he flew and sat up at the top of the tent and he sat there.
Yuŋkȟáŋ waná kȟoškálaka kiŋ tȟéhaŋl iyáyapi s’eléčheča kiŋ leháŋl, wíŋyaŋ k’uŋ hé wópȟaȟte gluǧáŋ na glužúžu.
And so then, it feeling like the young men had left to a long ways away, the woman opened up her bundle and unpacked it.
Hetáŋhaŋ šiná waŋ áta wičhápȟaha kšúpi čha glubláye!
From there she spread out a blanket all decorated with scalps.
Aglúta na heyé, “Hakéla pȟehíŋ kiŋ šikšíčela k’éyaš oyáya kiŋ éčuȟčiš uŋ-phíča séče ’uŋ,” eyíŋ na wókȟokipȟeya iȟát’é!
She stared at it and said, “Hakéla’s hair is pretty bad but you know at least the limbs might be useful,” and she laughed scarily.
Heyá naȟ’úŋ kiŋ wáŋčagna Hakéla čhiyéku kiŋ éowičhakiyaka čha glípi.
As soon as he heard her say that, Hakéla went to tell his big brothers that so they came back.
